and similarly for
\bar{L}_{\delta,x}
. In the sense of distributions, we also have
L_{N,x}\phi(y)=0
and
L_{N,x}\bar{\phi}(y)=G_{2N}(x,y)
,
where we use the regularisation function
\vartheta(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\mathrm{e}^{-x}
.

Using these differential operators, we obtain the following representation for the Wick ordered correlation functions.

Proposition 4.9
.

We have

(\widehat{\gamma}_{N,p})_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}=\frac{1}{\zeta}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bar{L}_{N,\tilde{x}_{1}}\cdots\bar{L}_{N,\tilde{x}_{p}}L_{N,x_{1}}\cdots L_{N,x_{p}}\mathrm{e}^{-V}\Bigr]\,.

The same statement holds for
V
replaced with
W^{\varepsilon}
.

Using this representation, we can show the following statement.

Proposition 4.10
.

Uniformly in
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{\tilde{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2p}
,

\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varepsilon\to 0\end{subarray}}(\widehat{\gamma}_{p}^{W^{\varepsilon}})_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}=(\widehat{\gamma}_{p})_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}\,.

(4.16)

Proof.

This statement follows from
(
7
, Lemma 4.10 and proof of (3.4))
, which we can adapt to our setting using techniques from Lemmas
4.1
and
4.2
.
∎

The proof of (
3.6
) follows from the following statement.

Lemma 4.11
.

Uniformly in
\varepsilon>0
,

\lvert(\widehat{\gamma}^{W^{\varepsilon}}_{p})_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}\rvert\lesssim_{v}\Upsilon_{\theta}(\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}})\,.

Proof of (
3.6
).

We can reproduce here the proof of
(
6
, Theorem 1.12)
.
From Proposition
4.10
and Lemma
4.11
, we have
\lvert(\widehat{\gamma}_{p})_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}\rvert\leqslant C\Upsilon_{\theta}(\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}})
. For any
c,\delta>0
, we can choose
R\equiv R(\delta)
such that

\sup_{\lvert\mathbf{x}\rvert+\lvert\tilde{\mathbf{x}}\rvert>R}\frac{\big|(\widehat{\gamma}_{p})_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}-(\widehat{\gamma}_{p}^{W^{\varepsilon}})_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}\big|}{\Upsilon_{\theta-c}(\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}})}\leqslant\sup_{\lvert\mathbf{x}\rvert+\lvert\tilde{\mathbf{x}}\rvert>R}\frac{C\Upsilon_{\theta}(\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}})}{\Upsilon_{\theta-c}(\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}})}\leqslant CR^{-c\theta}\leqslant\delta\,.

By Proposition
4.10
, we then have

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\sup_{\lvert\mathbf{x}\rvert+\lvert\tilde{\mathbf{x}}\rvert\leqslant R}\frac{\big|(\widehat{\gamma}_{p})_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}-(\widehat{\gamma}_{p}^{W^{\varepsilon}})_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}\big|}{\Upsilon_{\theta-c}(\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}})}=0\,.\qed

Proof of Proposition
4.11
.

Let
g(x)=1+\lvert x\rvert^{\theta}
chosen as in Assumption
2.1
.
Assume that
\varepsilon>0
small. Let
\ell\geqslant 0
and write

\mathcal{L}_{N,\mathbf{z}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=L_{N,z_{1}}^{\#}\cdots L_{N,z_{\ell}}^{\#}\,,

for
\mathbf{z}=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{\ell})\in\mathbb{R}^{2\ell}
, where
L^{\#}
stands for either
L
or
\bar{L}
.

Applying the Leibniz rule to the
2p
derivatives
L_{N,x_{i}}
and
\bar{L}_{N,\tilde{x}_{i}}
, we estimate
\lvert(\widehat{\gamma}^{W^{\varepsilon}}_{p})_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}\rvert
by a sum of finite number of terms of the form

\sup_{\mathbf{z}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{z}_{k}}\mathbb{E}\Big|\prod_{i=i}^{k}(\mathcal{L}_{N,\mathbf{z}_{i}}{W^{\varepsilon}})\mathrm{e}^{-{W^{\varepsilon}}}\Big|

where
\lceil p/2\rceil\leqslant k\leqslant 2p
and, for all
i=1,\cdots,k
,
\mathbf{z}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{2\ell_{i}}
for some
\ell_{i}\leqslant 4
. Notice that we have
\sum_{i}\ell_{i}\leqslant 2p
.
For
1\leqslant\ell\leqslant 4
, using Hölder’s inequality and hypercontractivity, we only need to show

\|\mathcal{L}_{N,\mathbf{y}}{W^{\varepsilon}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\Upsilon_{\theta}(\mathbf{y})\,.

(4.17)

Let us look at first order derivatives, that is, when
\ell=1
.
We start by considering

L_{N,y}W^{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}\,v^{\varepsilon}(u-{\tilde{u}})G_{2N}(u,y)\phi(u)(\phi({\tilde{u}})\phi({\tilde{u}})-G({\tilde{u}},{\tilde{u}}))\\
+\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}\,v^{\varepsilon}(u-{\tilde{u}})(\phi(u)\phi(u)-G(u,u))G_{2N}(y,{\tilde{u}})\phi({\tilde{u}})\\
-\int\mathrm{d}u\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}\,v^{\varepsilon}(u-{\tilde{u}})G(u,{\tilde{u}})G_{2N}(u,y)\phi(u)

(4.18)

and we can proceed in the same way for
\bar{L}_{N,y}W^{\varepsilon}
. We can then estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[({L_{N,y}W^{\varepsilon}})^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\tilde{g}(y)^{2}}

by using Proposition
7.1
and considering separately the cases where the integration variables
\hat{u}
are close (
\lvert\hat{u}-y\rvert\leqslant 1
) or far (
\lvert\hat{u}-y\rvert>1
) from
y
.
A direct computation shows that
\mathbb{E}\bigl[({L_{N,y}W^{\varepsilon}})^{2}\bigr]
is a sum of terms that can be grouped as

\int\mathrm{d}u_{1}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}_{1}\,\mathrm{d}u_{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}_{2}\,v^{\varepsilon}(u_{1}-\tilde{u}_{1})v^{\varepsilon}(u_{2}-\tilde{u}_{2})G_{2N}(a_{1},y)G_{2N}(a_{2},y)G(a_{3},a_{4})G(a_{5},a_{6})G(a_{7},a_{8})

(4.19)

where
\{a_{i}\}_{i=1}^{8}=\{u_{1},\tilde{u}_{1},u_{2},\tilde{u}_{2}\}
such that
\{a_{2i-1},a_{2i}\}\notin\{\{u_{1},\tilde{u}_{1}\},\{u_{2},\tilde{u}_{2}\}\}
and
a_{2i-1}\neq a_{2i}
for all
i=1,\dots,4

or

\int\mathrm{d}u_{1}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}_{1}\,\mathrm{d}u_{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}_{2}\,v^{\varepsilon}(u_{1}-\tilde{u}_{1})v^{\varepsilon}(u_{2}-\tilde{u}_{2})G_{2N}(a_{1},y)G_{2N}(a_{2},y)\\
\times G(u_{1},\tilde{u}_{1})G(u_{2},\tilde{u}_{2})(G(a_{3},a_{2})-G(a_{3},a_{4}))

(4.20)

where
a_{i}\in\{u_{1},\tilde{u}_{1},u_{2},\tilde{u}_{2}\}
such that
\{a_{1},a_{2}\}\notin\{\{u_{1},\tilde{u}_{1}\},\{u_{2},\tilde{u}_{2}\}\}
,
a_{3}\notin\{a_{2},a_{4}\}
, and
\{a_{2},a_{4}\}\in\{\{u_{1},\tilde{u}_{1}\},\{u_{2},\tilde{u}_{2}\}\}
.

We can deal with (
4.19
) using Young’s inequality for products and estimate

{G_{2N}(a_{2},y)}G(a_{3},a_{4})G(a_{5},a_{6})G(a_{7},a_{8})\\
\leqslant\frac{1}{4}\bigl(G(a_{2},y)^{4}+G(a_{3},a_{4})^{4}+G(a_{5},a_{6})^{4}+G(a_{7},a_{8})^{4}\bigr)\,

(4.21)

and split domains based on whether
a_{1}
or
a_{2}
belong to
B_{1}(y)
. Assume without loss of generality that
a_{1}=u_{1}
and
a_{2}=u_{2}
and note that
\{a_{2i-1},a_{2i}\}\cap\{u_{2},\tilde{u}_{2}\}\neq\emptyset
. Since
v^{\varepsilon}
has support in a ball of radius
\varepsilon
around the origin, then
\tilde{g}({u}_{i})\sim\tilde{g}(\tilde{u}_{i})
.
In the case where
a_{1},a_{2}\in B_{1}(y)
, we have that

\int_{B_{1}(y)}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}_{2}\,v^{\varepsilon}(u_{2}-\tilde{u}_{2})G(s,t)^{n}\leqslant\int_{B_{1}(y)}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}_{2}\,v^{\varepsilon}(u_{2}-\tilde{u}_{2})\log^{n}(\lvert s-t\rvert\sqrt{\tilde{g}(y)})^{-1}\lesssim\frac{1}{\tilde{g}(y)}

for any
s\in\{y,u_{1},\tilde{u}_{1}\}
,
t\in\{u_{2},\tilde{u}_{2}\}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
. This implies

\int_{{}_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{1}\in B_{1}(y)\\
u_{2}\in B_{1}(y)\end{subarray}}}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}_{1}\,\mathrm{d}u_{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}_{2}\,v^{\varepsilon}(u_{2}-{\tilde{u}_{2}})v^{\varepsilon}(u_{1}-{\tilde{u}_{1}})G(y,u_{1})G(s,t)^{n}\\
\lesssim\frac{1}{\tilde{g}(y)}\int_{u_{1}\in B_{1}(y)}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\,G(y,u_{1})\lesssim\frac{1}{\tilde{g}(y)^{2}}

for any
n>0
, and using the fact that
\widehat{v^{\varepsilon}}(0)=1
. The remaining cases can be treated analogously, using the fact that

\int_{B_{1}(y)^{c}}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}_{2}\,v^{\varepsilon}(u_{2}-\tilde{u}_{2})G(s,t)^{n}\lesssim\int_{B_{1}(y)^{c}}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}_{2}\,v^{\varepsilon}(u_{2}-\tilde{u}_{2})\mathrm{e}^{-\sqrt{\tilde{g}(y)}\lvert s-t\rvert}\lesssim\frac{1}{\tilde{g}(y)}\,.

The term in (
4.20
) requires a more detailed analysis, as it contains an integration over the diagonal of
G
and
\int\mathrm{d}y\,v^{\varepsilon}(y)G(x,x+y)\sim\log(\varepsilon^{-1})
. Let us assume that
a_{2}=u_{1},a_{4}=\tilde{u}_{1}
. Performing a change of variables
z=(a_{4}-a_{2})/\varepsilon

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:diffinfterms}

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\lambda\int\mathrm{d}u_{1}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}_{1}\,\mathrm{d}u_{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}_{2}\,v(z)v^{\varepsilon}(u_{2}-\tilde{u}_{2})G(a_{1},y)G(u_{1},y)

(4.22)

\displaystyle\times G(u_{1},\tilde{u}_{1})G(u_{2},\tilde{u}_{2})\frac{\varepsilon\lvert z\rvert\mathrm{e}^{-\sqrt{\tilde{g}(a_{3})}\lvert a_{3}-(u_{1}+(1-\lambda)\varepsilon\lvert z\rvert)\rvert}}{\lvert a_{3}-(u_{1}+(1-\lambda)\varepsilon{z})\rvert}

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\lambda\int\mathrm{d}u_{1}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}_{1}\,\mathrm{d}u_{2}\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}_{2}\,v(z)v^{\varepsilon}(u_{2}-\tilde{u}_{2})G(a_{1},y)G(u_{1},y)

\displaystyle\times G(u_{2},\tilde{u}_{2})\frac{\varepsilon^{1-c}\lvert z\rvert^{1-c}\mathrm{e}^{-\sqrt{\tilde{g}(a_{3})}\lvert a_{3}-(u_{1}+(1-\lambda)\varepsilon\lvert z\rvert)\rvert}}{\lvert a_{3}-(u_{1}+(1-\lambda)\varepsilon{z})\rvert}

for any
c>0
. By splitting cases and using the fact that
G(y,u_{1})G(y,a_{1})\lesssim\frac{1}{2}(G(y,u_{1})^{2}+G(y,a_{1})^{2})
, we obtain

\eqref{eq:secondtermgammaWeps}\lesssim\varepsilon^{1-c}\frac{1}{\tilde{g}(y)^{2}}

for any
c>0
.

We can follow the arguments above for
\ell=2
and we obtain

\displaystyle\|\bar{L}_{N,y_{2}}L_{N,y_{1}}{W^{\varepsilon}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{v}\Upsilon_{\theta}(y_{1},y_{2})

by considering the cases
|y_{1}-y_{2}|\leqslant 2
and
|y_{1}-y_{2}|>2
separately.

In order to show

\|{L}_{N,y_{2}}L_{N,y_{1}}{W^{\varepsilon}}\|_{L^{2}}\vee\|\bar{L}_{N,y_{2}}\bar{L}_{N,y_{1}}{W^{\varepsilon}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\Upsilon_{\theta}(y_{1},y_{2})

and the corresponding results when
\ell>2
, it is only necessary to bound terms of the form (
4.20
), and we obtain (
4.17
) by proceeding as in (
4.19
).
∎

5. Functional integral representations

In this section we derive the functional integral representation underlying the proof of Proposition
3.1
(see also the overview in Section
3
). In the following two propositions, Propositions
5.3
and
5.6
, we give the functional integral representation of the (quantum)
relative partition function
\mathcal{Z}
from (
2.14
) and the (classical) relative partition function
\zeta^{W^{\varepsilon}}
corresponding
to the interaction
W^{\varepsilon}
.

Before starting, we recall the auxiliary fields used in
6
to derive the functional integral representation.
Let us fix a function
\varphi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
which is even, non-negative, of positive type, and which satisfies
\varphi(0)=1
. For given
\eta>0
, we define a positive definite function on the interval
[0,\nu)
as

\delta_{\eta,\nu}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\frac{1}{\eta}\sum_{y\in\mathbb{Z}}\check{\varphi}\Bigl(\frac{x-\nu y}{\eta}\Bigr)\,,

(5.1)

such that
\int\mathrm{d}x\,\delta_{\eta,\nu}(x)=1
, where
\check{\varphi}
denotes the inverse Fourier transform of
\varphi
. The function
\delta_{\eta,\nu}
can be interpreted as an approximate delta function on
[0,\nu)
.
For
\eta>0
, we introduce a centred real Gaussian field
\sigma\vcentcolon[0,\nu)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
with covariance kernel

C_{\eta}(\tau,\tilde{\tau};x,\tilde{x})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\delta_{\nu,\eta}(\tau-\tilde{\tau})\varphi(\eta x)v^{\varepsilon}(x-\tilde{x})\varphi(\eta\tilde{x})

whose law we denote with
\mu_{C_{\eta}}
. We denote its time average as

\xi(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\frac{1}{\nu}\int_{0}^{\nu}\mathrm{d}\tau\,\sigma(\tau,x)

whose law is
\mu_{v^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}
, a centred Gaussian with covariance kernel

v^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x,\tilde{x})=\varphi(\eta x)v^{\varepsilon}(x-\tilde{x})\varphi(\eta\tilde{x})\,.

Note that the fact that
v
is smooth and the use of the spatial cutoff through
\varphi
ensure that the field
\xi
is smooth and bounded almost surely for every
\varepsilon,\eta>0
. We need the following assumption on a family of functions
(\alpha^{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
as introduced in Section
3
.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
U
satisfy Assumption
2.1
and
v
satisfy Assumption
2.5
. For each
\varepsilon>0
we choose a nonnegative function
\alpha^{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that, uniformly for all
\eta\geqslant 0
small enough,

U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)=(U+\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})(x)\geqslant\frac{1}{2}U(x)\,,

(5.2)

\lim_{\eta\to 0}\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}=0\,,

(5.3)

and

\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}}\vee\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|\tau^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}\,.

(5.4)

Here we recall the definition of
\alpha_{\eta}^{\varepsilon}
from (
3.14
).

Remark 5.2
.

A function
\alpha^{\varepsilon}
as in Definition
5.1
can be easily constructed. For instance, we can choose
\alpha^{\varepsilon}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=(\|\tau^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}}+1)\mathds{1}_{\bar{B}_{r_{\varepsilon}}}
, where
B_{r}
is a ball of radius
r
centred at 0 and
r_{\varepsilon}>0
is chosen appropriately.

From Definition
5.1
, more precisely from (
5.2
), it follows that
(-\Delta/2+\kappa+U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta})^{-1}
is Hilbert-Schmidt. Indeed, by the Feynman-Kac formula (see Lemma
5.11
below) we have

\mathrm{e}^{-t{h^{U}}}(x,y)\vee\mathrm{e}^{-t{h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}}}(x,y)<\mathrm{e}^{-t{h^{U/2}}}(x,y)

(5.5)

for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
t>0
and uniformly in
\varepsilon>0,\eta\geqslant 0
small enough. Since, for
h,r>0

\frac{1}{h^{r}}=\frac{1}{\Gamma(r)}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,{\mathrm{e}^{-ht}}\,t^{r-1}\,,

(
5.5
) implies that

\tr{\frac{1}{(h^{U})^{s}}}\vee\tr{\frac{1}{(h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}})^{s}}}\leqslant\tr{\frac{1}{(h^{U/2})^{s}}}

(5.6)

which is finite whenever
\tr{\bigl(h^{U}\bigr)^{-s}}<\infty
.

We have the following functional integral representation of the relative partition function
\zeta^{W^{\varepsilon}}
.

Proposition 5.3
.

Suppose that Assumption
2.1
and Assumption
2.5
hold. Let
\alpha^{\varepsilon}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
be the function as in Definition
5.1
.

(i)

The partition function
\zeta^{W^{\varepsilon}}=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-W^{\varepsilon}}\bigr]
is given by

\zeta^{W^{\varepsilon}}=\lim_{\eta\to 0}\mathrm{e}^{E^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}x\,\alpha^{\varepsilon}(x)\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)+S^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}\int\mu_{v^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\xi)\,\mathrm{e}^{-i\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}+\alpha^{\varepsilon},\xi\rangle}\mathrm{e}^{f_{2}(\xi)}\,,

(5.7)

where we defined

f_{2}(\xi)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\tr\biggl(\frac{1}{t+h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}-\mathrm{i}\xi}-\frac{1}{t+h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}}-\frac{1}{t+h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}}\mathrm{i}\xi\frac{1}{t+h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}}\biggr)\,,

(5.8)

S_{\eta}^{\varepsilon}=\log\mathbb{E}\biggl[{\exp\Bigl(-\int\mathrm{d}x\,(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)-\tau^{\varepsilon}(x))\,{\vcentcolon\!\lvert\phi(x)\rvert^{2}\!\vcentcolon}\Bigr)}\biggr]\,,

(5.9)

and

T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\biggl(\frac{1}{\kappa-\Delta/2+U}-\frac{1}{\kappa-\Delta/2+U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}\biggr)(x,x)\,.

(5.10)

Here
\xi
is interpreted as a multiplication operator.

(ii)

The function
f_{2}(\xi)
is finite and has non-positive real part.

We can obtain a similar expression for a regularised version of the quantum relative partition function
\mathcal{Z}
from (
2.14
). In order to do it, we introduce the following definitions.

Definition 5.4
.

Let
b
an operator on
L^{2}([0,\nu]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
acting on functions
f\in L^{2}([0,\nu)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
of time
\tau\in[0,\nu)
and space
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We use the notation
b^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}_{x,\tilde{x}}
for the operator kernel of
b
. Thus

(bf)(\tau,x)=\int\mathrm{d}\tau\,\int\mathrm{d}x\,b^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}_{x,\tilde{x}}f(\tau,x)\,.

The following operator plays an important role in the rest of the analysis.

Definition 5.5
.

Let
I\in\mathbb{R}
be a closed bounded interval and
V:I\times{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\to[0,\infty)
a locally bounded function, and suppose that
\re V\leqslant-c
for some positive constant
c
. On
L^{2}([0,\nu)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
define the operator

K(V)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\partial_{\tau}-\frac{\Delta}{2}-V(\tau)\,,

with periodic boundary conditions in
[0,\nu)
.

Moreover, for a bounded operator
b
on
L^{2}([0,\nu)\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
with continuous operator kernel
b^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}_{x,\tilde{x}}
, we define

\Tr(b)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\int_{0}^{\nu}\mathrm{d}\tau\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{d}x\,b^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}_{x,\tilde{x}}\,;

see also
(
8
, Definition 3.10)
for more details.

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
H
be defined as in (
2.18
), and
U
satisfy Assumption
2.1
, and
v^{\varepsilon}
Assumption
2.5
. Moreover, let
K
be defined as in Definition
5.5
. Then the following holds for fixed
\varepsilon,\nu>0
.

(i)

We have

\mathcal{Z}=\lim_{\eta\to 0}\mathrm{e}^{E^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}x\,\alpha^{\varepsilon}(x)\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)+{S^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}}}\int\mu_{C_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\sigma)\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\langle\sigma,\alpha^{\varepsilon}+{T^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}}\rangle_{\nu}}\mathrm{e}^{F_{2}(\sigma)}\,,

(5.11)

where we defined

F_{2}(\sigma)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\Tr\biggl(\frac{1}{t+K(-\kappa-U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}+\mathrm{i}\sigma)}-\frac{1}{t+K(-\kappa-U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta})}\\
-\frac{1}{t+K(-\kappa-U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta})}\mathrm{i}\sigma\frac{1}{t+K(-\kappa-U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta})}\biggr)\,,

(5.12)

S^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\Tr\biggl(\frac{1}{t+K(-\kappa-U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta})}-\frac{1}{t+K(-\kappa-U)}\biggr)+\big\langle\varrho_{\nu}^{U},\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\big\rangle\,,

(5.13)

and

T^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\varrho_{\nu}^{U}(x)-\varrho_{\nu}^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}(x)\,.

(5.14)

Here
\sigma
is interpreted as a multiplication operator.

(ii)

The function
F_{2}
is finite and has non-positive real part.

Notice that, from the representation in
(
6
, Lemma 5.4)
, it follows that (
5.12
) is finite. From the discussion in Section
6.1
, we can show that (
5.13
) and (
5.14
) are also bounded quantities using a similar Brownian bridge representation.

From Propositions
5.3
and
5.6
, we can establish functional integral representations for the Wick-ordered
p
-point correlation functions similar to
(
7
, Lemmas 5.18 and 5.19)
. This shows (
3.4
). For conciseness, we will not provide further details.

We split the proofs of Propositions
5.3
and
5.6
in two subsections.

5.1.  Proof of Proposition
5.3

The aim of this subsection is to prove Proposition
5.3
, by deriving a functional integral representation for the classical field theory
W^{\varepsilon}
. We first show that the constant (
5.9
) is uniformly bounded in
\eta
and that the function (
5.10
) is well-defined, integrable and bounded. We use then these results to arrive at the proof of Proposition
5.3
, stating whenever we can exploit directly results from
(
6
, Proposition 4.1)
.

Throughout the subsection we use the regularised version of the field
W^{\varepsilon}
, defined in (
3.12
). The following lemma, whose proof is analogous to
(
4
, proof of Lemma 1.5)
, ensures that the field
W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}
is well defined.

Lemma 5.7
.

Suppose that Assumption
2.1
, Assumption
2.5
, and Remark
2.3
hold. Let
W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}
be defined as in (
3.12
). Then, uniformly in
\eta\geqslant 0
and for every
\varepsilon>0
, the sequence
\bigl(W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}\bigr)_{N>0}

is a Cauchy sequence in
\bigcap_{m\geqslant 1}L^{m}(\mathbb{P})
. We denote its limit by
W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}
. Moreover, for all fixed
\varepsilon>0
,

\lim_{\eta\to 0}W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}=W^{\varepsilon}_{0}\equiv W^{\varepsilon}

in
\bigcap_{m\geqslant 1}L^{m}(\mathbb{P})
.

In the following, we will use the notation

P_{N}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbbold{1}_{\lambda_{k}\leqslant N}u_{k}u_{k}^{*}\,

for the projection onto a finite subspace of
\mathcal{H}
spanned by the eigenvectors of
h

and define

(b)_{N}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=P_{N}bP_{N}\,

for any operator
b
acting on
\mathcal{H}
.

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}
be defined as in (
3.14
), where
\alpha^{\varepsilon}
satisfies Definition
5.1
.
Then, for a fixed
\varepsilon>0
, and for all
\eta\geqslant 0
small enough, we have

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[{\mathrm{e}^{-\int\mathrm{d}x\,(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)-\tau^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x))\vcentcolon\lvert\phi_{N}(x)\rvert^{2}\vcentcolon}}\Bigr]=\mathbb{E}\Bigl[{\mathrm{e}^{-\int\mathrm{d}x\,(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)-\tau^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x))\vcentcolon\lvert\phi(x)\rvert^{2}\vcentcolon}}\Bigr]<\infty\,.

(5.15)

Proof.

The convergence of the limit in (
5.15
)
follows from Lemma
7.6
, Definition
5.1
applied to
f\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}
, and hypercontractivity (see
(
7
, Appendix A.2)
for further details). The argument, as in
(
7
, lemma 4.6)
, can be summarised in the following steps.

1.

I_{N}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\int\mathrm{d}x\,(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)-\tau^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)){\vcentcolon\!\lvert\phi_{N}(x)\rvert^{2}\!\vcentcolon}
and
I\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\int\mathrm{d}x\,(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)-\tau^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)){\vcentcolon\!\lvert\phi(x)\rvert^{2}\!\vcentcolon}
belong to the second polynomial chaos, using the fact that both quantities are orthogonal to the generators
{\vcentcolon\!\phi(f_{1})\cdots\phi(f_{n})\!\vcentcolon}
,
f_{i}\in\mathcal{H}^{-1}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=D(h^{-1/2})
, of the
n
-th polynomial chaos whenever
n\neq 2
.
This follows from the fact that

{\vcentcolon\!\phi(f_{1})\cdots\phi(f_{n})\!\vcentcolon}=\int\mathrm{d}x_{1}\cdots\,\mathrm{d}x_{n}\,f(x_{1})\cdots f(x_{n}){\vcentcolon\!\phi(x_{1})\cdots\phi(x_{n})\!\vcentcolon}

and that, when
n\neq m

\mathbb{E}\bigl[{\vcentcolon\!\phi(x_{1})\cdots\phi(x_{m})\!\vcentcolon}\,{\vcentcolon\!\phi(x_{1})\cdots\phi(x_{n})\!\vcentcolon}\bigr]=0\,.

2.

We can thus show that

\|\mathrm{e}^{-I_{N}}\|_{L^{p}}<\infty\iff\|\mathrm{e}^{-I}\|_{L^{p}}<\infty\,.

(5.16)

By step 1, we can estimate

\big\|\mathrm{e}^{I_{N}-I}-1\big\|_{L^{p}}\leqslant\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\frac{1}{k!}\big\|(I_{N}-I)^{k}\big\|_{L^{p}}=\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\frac{1}{k!}\big\|I_{N}-I\big\|_{L^{pk}}^{k}\,.

(5.17)

Now we use the hypercontractivity of the
k
moments and that
I_{N}-I
is in the second polynomial chaos, as well as the Stirling approximation in the last inequality

\eqref{hypercontract_2}\leqslant\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\frac{1}{k!}(pk)^{k}\big\|I_{N}-I\big\|_{L^{2}}^{k}\leqslant\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\bigl(Cp\|I_{N}-I\|_{L^{2}}\bigr)^{k}.

(5.18)

Hence, (
5.18
) converges for
N
large enough from an argument similar to the proof of Lemma
5.7
. Indeed, the result in Lemma
5.7
can also be extended to show that
\int\mathrm{d}x\,f(x)\,{\vcentcolon\!|\phi_{N}(x)|^{2}\!\vcentcolon}
is a Cauchy sequence in
L^{m}
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
as long as
\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}<\infty
. This proves (
5.16
).

We can now conclude the proof by showing

\mathbb{E}\biggl[{\exp\biggl({-\int\mathrm{d}x\,(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)-\tau^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x))\,{\vcentcolon\!\lvert\phi_{N}(x)\rvert^{2}\!\vcentcolon}}}\biggr)\biggr]<\infty

uniformly in
N
for a given choice of regularisation function
\vartheta
.

Let us consider the regularisation function
\vartheta(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\mathbbold{1}_{x\leqslant 1}
. For any
N>0
, we define

\phi_{N}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}X_{k}\sqrt{\frac{\vartheta(\lambda_{k}/N)}{\lambda_{k}}}u_{k}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}X_{k}\frac{\mathbbold{1}_{\lambda_{k}\leqslant N}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{k}}}u_{k}=P_{N}\phi.

(5.19)

Then
P_{N}\phi
is a finite dimensional centred Gaussian vector with covariance
P_{N}h^{-1}
. As in the proof of
(
6
, Proposition 4.1)
, we have

\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int\mathrm{d}x\,(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)-\tau^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x))\vcentcolon\lvert\phi_{N}(x)\rvert^{2}\vcentcolon}\Bigr]\\
=\exp\biggl\{\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\tr\biggl({P_{N}\biggl(\frac{1}{t+h}\bigl(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\bigr)_{N}\frac{1}{t+h}\bigl(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\bigr)_{N}\frac{1}{t+h+\bigl(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\bigr)_{N}}\biggr)}\biggr)\biggr\}\,.

(5.20)

To conclude, we want to show that the right-hand side of (
5.20
) is bounded.
By Assumption
2.1
and (
5.6
), using
\|(\tau^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta})_{N}\|_{\mathfrak{S}^{\infty}}\leqslant\|\tau^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}}
and Hölder’s inequality, we get

\displaystyle\lvert\log{\eqref{eq:trace_pho_tau}}\rvert\leqslant\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\Big\|\frac{1}{t+{h^{U/2}}}\Big\|_{\mathfrak{S}^{3}}^{3}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\tr\Bigl\{\Bigl(\frac{1}{t+h^{U/2}}\Bigr)^{3}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\tr\Bigl\{\bigl({h^{U/2}}\bigr)^{-2}\Bigr\}<\infty\,.\qed

Let us the define the operator
\mathcal{T}^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}
acting on
\mathcal{H}
as

\mathcal{T}^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\frac{1}{h^{U}}-\frac{1}{h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}}\,.

(5.21)

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
U
satisfy Assumption
2.1
and
\alpha^{\varepsilon}
as in Definition
5.1
. Consider
\mathcal{T}^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}
as in (
5.21
). Then,

(i)

the operator
\mathcal{T}^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}

is trace-class and has a continuous kernel for any
\eta\geqslant 0
small enough. The same statement holds for its restriction to the span of a finite number of eigenvectors of
h

\mathcal{T}^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=P_{N}\mathcal{T}^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}P_{N}\,.

(5.22)

(ii)

The diagonal of the operator
\mathcal{T}^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}
, denoted by
T_{\eta,N}^{\varepsilon}(x)\equiv\mathcal{T}_{\eta,N}(x,x)
, converges in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
to
T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)
. Moreover,
T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

(iii)

Uniformly in
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\lim_{\eta\to 0}T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)=T^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x)=\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}T^{\varepsilon}(x)\,.

Proof.

(i) Consider the operator
\mathcal{T}^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}
as in (
5.22
) whose kernel we denote by
\mathcal{T}^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x,y)
for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. By a resolvent expansion and Hölder’s inequality on Schatten spaces, we find that
\mathcal{T}^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}
is trace-class, since

\|\mathcal{T}^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\|_{\mathfrak{S}_{1}}\leqslant\Big\|\frac{1}{h^{U}}\Big\|_{\mathfrak{S}_{2}}\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big\|\frac{1}{h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}}\Big\|_{\mathfrak{S}_{2}}<\infty

uniformly, for
\eta\geqslant 0
small enough.

Let us now show the continuity of
T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x,y)
at all points
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Since the kernel of
1/h^{U}
,
1/h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}
is continuous outside the set of measure zero
\{(x,x)\vcentcolon x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
and

{\mathcal{T}^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x,y)=\int\mathrm{d}w\,\frac{1}{h^{U}}(x,w)\,({\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(w)-\tau^{\varepsilon}(w)})\,\frac{1}{h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}}(w,y)}\,,

we can conclude by dominated convergence that
(x,y)\mapsto T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x,y)
is jointly continuous.

Let us now consider the operator
\mathcal{T}^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=P_{N}\mathcal{T}^{\varepsilon}P_{N}
defined in (
5.22
). Arguing as above and using the fact that

\Big\|(1-P_{N})\frac{1}{h^{U}}\Big\|_{\mathfrak{S}_{2}}^{2}=\sum_{k\geqslant 0}\frac{\mathbbold{1}_{\lambda_{k}>N}}{\lambda_{k}^{2}}\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}0,

we also have that
\mathcal{T}^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}
is a trace-class operator for all
N>0
, and its kernel

\mathcal{T}^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}(x,y)=\sum_{n,m\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbbold{1}_{\lambda_{n}\vee\lambda_{m}\leqslant N}\langle u_{l},\mathcal{T}^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}u_{m}\rangle u_{m}(x)\bar{u}_{n}(y)

is continuous since it is a finite sum of continuous functions.

(ii) Let
\{\mu_{k}\}_{k\geqslant 0}
denote the singular values of
T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}
,
\{\psi_{k}\}_{k\geqslant 0}
the corresponding singular vectors and
\{\phi_{k}\}_{k\geqslant 0}
a family of orthonormal vectors defined as
\phi_{k}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\mu_{k}^{-1}T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\psi_{k}
for all
k\geqslant 0
. Then, following the argument in
(
1
, Section 1)
together with the continuity of
T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x,y)
, we have

(\mathcal{T}^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}f)(x)=\sum_{k\geqslant 0}\mu_{k}\langle f,\psi_{k}\rangle\phi_{k}(x)

(5.23)

and the series expansion

\sum_{k\geqslant 0}\mu_{k}\phi_{k}(x)\bar{\psi}_{k}(x)

converges for almost every point in the diagonal of
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
to
T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)
. The series expansion
(
5.23
)
and the argument above imply that

T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}(x)=\sum_{k\geqslant 0}\mu_{k}P_{N}\phi_{k}(x)P_{N}\overline{\psi}_{k}(x)

for almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Using Cauchy-Schwarz we find

\begin{split}\int\mathrm{d}x\,\lvert T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)-T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}(x)\rvert&\leqslant\sum_{k\geqslant 0}\mu_{k}\biggl(\int\mathrm{d}x\,\lvert(1-P_{N})\phi_{k}(x)\bar{\psi}_{k}(x)\rvert\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt+\int\mathrm{d}x\,\lvert P_{N}\phi_{k}(x)(1-P_{N})\bar{\psi}_{k}(x)\rvert\biggr)\\
&\leqslant\sum_{k=0}^{M}\mu_{k}\bigl(\|(1-P_{N})\phi_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|(1-P_{N})\psi_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\bigr)+\sum_{k>M}\mu_{k}\,.\end{split}

(5.24)

the right-hand side of (
5.24
) converges to 0 as
M,N\to\infty
, since for any
\varepsilon>0
, we can choose
M>0
big enough such the second term of (
5.24
) is smaller than
\varepsilon
, as
\mathcal{T}^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}
is Hermitian and
\|\mathcal{T}^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\|_{\mathfrak{G}^{2}}<\infty
, and set
N>0
large enough such that
\lvert(1-P_{N})\phi_{k}\rvert+\lvert(1-P_{N})\psi_{k}\rvert\leqslant\varepsilon
for all
k=0,\dots,M
.

(iii) By a resolvent expansion and the bounds on
(\kappa-\Delta)^{-1}
, which follow from Proposition
7.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lvert T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)-T^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x)\rvert

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}y\,\frac{1}{h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{0}}}(x,y)\lvert\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)-\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x)\rvert\frac{1}{h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}}(y,x)\leqslant\kappa^{d/2-2}\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

The uniform convergence for
\eta\to 0
follows from the limit (
5.3
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.3
.

We regularise the interaction
W^{\varepsilon}
replacing it with
W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}
as in (
3.12
), so that

\zeta^{W^{\varepsilon}}=\lim_{\eta\to 0}\lim_{N\to\infty}\zeta^{W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}}

where we use the regularisation function
\vartheta(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\mathbbold{1}_{\lvert x\rvert\leqslant 1}
as in (
5.19
).
Indeed, by Lemma
5.7
, we have

\lim_{\eta\to 0}\lim_{N\to\infty}\big\|W^{\varepsilon}-W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}\big\|_{L^{q}}\leqslant\lim_{\eta\to 0}\big\|W^{\varepsilon}-W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\big\|_{L^{q}}+\lim_{N\to\infty}\big\|W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}\big\|_{L^{q}}=0

for any
1\leqslant q<\infty
.
We then have for any
1\leqslant p<\infty
and
r=pq/(q-p)

\displaystyle\big\|\mathrm{e}^{-W^{\varepsilon}}-\mathrm{e}^{-W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}}\big\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,\big\|(W^{\varepsilon}-W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N})\,\mathrm{e}^{-tW^{\varepsilon}-(1-t)W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}}\big\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\big\|W^{\varepsilon}-W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}\big\|_{L^{q}}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,\big\|t\mathrm{e}^{-W^{\varepsilon}}+(1-t)\mathrm{e}^{-W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}}\big\|_{L^{r}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\big\|W^{\varepsilon}-W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}\big\|_{L^{q}}\,,

where we used the integrability of
W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}
(cf. Section
4
) and

\zeta^{W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}}<\infty\,,

which can be seen from the identity (
5.29
) below
and that the fact that
\re{f_{2,N}}<0
for any
N>0
.
Let us now show

\zeta^{W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}=\mathrm{e}^{E^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}x\,\alpha^{\varepsilon}(x)\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)+S_{\eta}}\int\mu_{v^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\xi)\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}+\alpha^{\varepsilon},\xi\rangle}\mathrm{e}^{f_{2}(\xi)}\,.

Introducing a zero in the exponent and rearranging the terms, we find

\zeta^{W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}}=\mathrm{e}^{E^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}x\,\alpha^{\varepsilon}(x)\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)}\\
\times\mathbb{E}\biggl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int\mathrm{d}x\,\bigl(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)-\tau^{\varepsilon}(x)\bigr):\lvert\phi_{N}(x)\rvert^{2}:-\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\,\bigl(\lvert\phi_{N}(x)\rvert^{2}-\alpha^{\varepsilon}(x)\bigr)\varphi(\eta x)v^{\varepsilon}(x-\tilde{x})\varphi(\eta\tilde{x})\bigl(\lvert\phi_{N}(\tilde{x})\rvert^{2}-\alpha^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x})\bigr)}\biggr]\,.

(5.25)

Since

\mathbb{E}\bigl[\langle\phi_{N}(x),\phi_{N}(y)\rangle\bigr]=(h^{-1})_{N}(x,y)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathbbold{1}_{\lambda_{k}\leqslant N}}{\lambda_{k}}u_{k}(x)u_{k}^{*}(y)\,,

\phi_{N}
is a finite-dimensional complex Gaussian with covariance
(h^{-1})_{N}
. We then find by a direct computation

\mathrm{e}^{-\int\mathrm{d}x\,(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)-\tau^{\varepsilon}(x))\vcentcolon\lvert\phi_{N}(x)\rvert^{2}\vcentcolon}\,\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{d}\phi)=\mathrm{e}^{S^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}}\,\mathbb{P}^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\phi)\,,

(5.26)

where

S^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\log\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int\mathrm{d}x\,(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)-\tau^{\varepsilon}(x))\,:\lvert\phi_{N}(x)\rvert^{2}:}\Bigr]

is uniformly bounded in
N>0
and converges by Lemma
5.8
. Similarly, for a centred Gaussian field
\phi
covariance of covariance
\bigl(h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}\bigr)^{-1}
, we have

\mathbb{E}^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}\bigl[\phi_{N}(x)\overline{\phi_{N}}(y)\bigr]=\Bigl(\frac{1}{h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}}\Bigr)_{N}(x,y)\,.

Hence,

\eqref{w_n:introductionofzero}=\mathrm{e}^{S^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}+E^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}x\,\alpha^{\varepsilon}(x)\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)}\\
\times\mathbb{E}^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\,\bigl(:\lvert\phi_{N}(x)\rvert^{2}:_{h^{U}}-\alpha^{\varepsilon}(x)\bigr)\varphi(\eta x)v^{\varepsilon}(x,\tilde{x})\varphi(\eta\tilde{x})\bigl(\vcentcolon\lvert\phi_{N}(\tilde{x})\rvert^{2}\vcentcolon_{h^{U}}-\alpha^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x})\bigr)}\Bigr]

(5.27)

where the Wick ordering

{\vcentcolon\!|\phi_{N}(x)|^{2}\!\vcentcolon}_{h^{U}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=|\phi_{N}(x)|^{2}-\bigl(h^{U}\bigr)^{-1}_{N}(x,x)

is now performed with respect to a covariance different from that of the law of
\phi_{N}
. In order to arrive at the expression of
f_{2}
in (
5.8
), we need to Wick order with respect to the law of
\phi
in (
5.27
). Thus, we replace
{\vcentcolon\!\lvert\phi_{N}(x)^{2}\rvert\!\vcentcolon}_{h^{U}}
by
{\vcentcolon\!\lvert\phi_{N}(x)^{2}\rvert\!\vcentcolon}_{h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}}
by subtracting

T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}(x)=\biggl(\frac{P_{N}}{h^{U}}-\frac{P_{N}}{h^{U}+(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})_{N}}\biggr)(x,x)\,.

Thus, we rewrite

\penalty\ \eqref{eq:w_n:change of law}=\mathrm{e}^{S^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}+E^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}x\,\alpha^{\varepsilon}(x)\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)}\\
\times\mathbb{E}^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}\biggl[\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\,\bigl(\vcentcolon|\phi_{N}(x)|^{2}\vcentcolon-\alpha^{\varepsilon}(x)-T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}(x)\bigr)\varphi(\eta x)v^{\varepsilon}(x-\tilde{x})\varphi(\eta\tilde{x})\bigl(\vcentcolon|\phi_{N}(\tilde{x})|^{2}\vcentcolon-\alpha^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x})-T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}(\tilde{x})\bigr)}\biggr]\,,

(5.28)

where the Wick ordering is done with respect to the measure
\mathbb{P}^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}
.
The rest of the proof follows the one of
(
6
, Proposition 4.1)
. We present here the main ideas.

Applying a Hubbard-Stratonovich transformation (
3.7
) with
f(x)={\vcentcolon\!\lvert\phi_{N}(x)\rvert^{2}\!\vcentcolon}-\alpha^{\varepsilon}(x)-T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}(x)
and using the notation
\varrho_{N}(x)=\big(\frac{P_{N}}{h+(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})_{N}}\big)(x,x)
, we find

\eqref{final_step_of_f_2}=\mathrm{e}^{S^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}+E^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}x\,\alpha(x)\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)-\mathrm{i}\langle\xi,\alpha^{\varepsilon}+T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}\rangle}\int\mu_{v^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\xi)\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\langle\xi,\varrho_{N}\rangle}\mathbb{E}^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\langle\phi_{N},\xi\phi_{N}\rangle}\Bigr]\,.

Moreover, we find the expression

\displaystyle\begin{split}{f_{2,N}(\xi)}&\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\log\biggl\{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\langle\xi,\varrho_{N}\rangle}\mathbb{E}^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\langle\phi_{N},\xi\phi_{N}\rangle}\Bigr]\biggr\}\\
&=-\mathrm{i}\langle\xi,\varrho_{N}\rangle+\log{\mathbb{E}^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\langle\phi_{N},\,\xi\phi_{N}\rangle}\Bigr]}\\
&=-\mathrm{i}\langle\xi,\varrho_{N}\rangle+\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\tr\biggl[P_{N}\biggl(\frac{1}{t+h^{U}+(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}-\mathrm{i}\xi)_{N}}{}-\frac{1}{t+h^{U}+(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})_{N}}\biggr)\biggr]\\
&=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\tr\biggl[P_{N}\biggl(\frac{1}{t+h^{U}+(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}-\mathrm{i}\xi)_{N}}{}-\frac{1}{t+h^{U}+(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})_{N}}\\
&\qquad-\frac{1}{t+h^{U}+(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})_{N}}\mathrm{i}\xi_{N}\frac{1}{t+h^{U}+(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})_{N}}\biggr)\biggr]\,.\end{split}

Hence, we can rewrite the truncated partition function
\zeta^{W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}}
as

\zeta^{W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}}=\mathrm{e}^{E^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}x\,\alpha^{\varepsilon}(x)\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)+S^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}}\int\mu_{v^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\xi)\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}+\alpha^{\varepsilon},\xi\rangle}\mathrm{e}^{f_{2,N}(\xi)}\,.

(5.29)

Finally, we notice that
\mathrm{Re}\bigl\{\bigl(-\mathrm{i}\langle\xi,\alpha^{\varepsilon}+T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}\rangle+f_{2,N}(\xi)\bigr)\bigr\},\re{(-\mathrm{i}\langle\xi,\alpha^{\varepsilon}+T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\rangle+f_{2}(\xi))}<0
where
f_{2}(\xi)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\lim_{N\to\infty}f_{2,N}(\xi)
exists almost surely since
\xi
is almost surely bounded (see
(
6
, Proof of Proposition 4.1.)
for further details).

Lastly, we want to show the convergence of
\zeta^{W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}}
to
\zeta^{W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}
as
N\to\infty
. First using Cauchy-Schwarz, recalling (
2.11
) and the fact that
\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}<\infty
since
\varphi
has compact support, we show

\displaystyle\biggl(\int\mu_{v^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\xi)|\langle\xi,T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}\rangle|\biggr)^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant\int\mu_{v^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\xi)\big|\langle\xi,T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}\rangle\big|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y\,(T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N})(x)v^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x-y)(T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N})(y)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}\|_{L^{1}}\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}0\,,

(5.30)

where in the last line we used Young’s convolution inequality and Lemma
5.9
(ii). Hence, using dominated convergence, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\Big|{\zeta^{W^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}}}-\mathrm{e}^{E^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}x\,\alpha^{\varepsilon}(x)\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)+S^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}\int\mu_{v^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\xi)\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}+\alpha^{\varepsilon},\xi\rangle}\mathrm{e}^{f_{2}(\xi)}\Big|

\displaystyle\,\leqslant\lim_{N\to\infty}\Big|\mathrm{e}^{S^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}}-\mathrm{e}^{S^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}\Big|\mathrm{e}^{E^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}x\,\alpha^{\varepsilon}(x)\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)}\int\mu_{v^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\xi)\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\langle T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}+\alpha^{\varepsilon},\xi\rangle}\mathrm{e}^{f_{2}(\xi)}

\displaystyle\;\;+\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathrm{e}^{E^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}x\,\alpha^{\varepsilon}(x)\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)+S^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}\int\mu_{v^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\xi)\,\big|\bigl(-\mathrm{i}\langle\xi,\,T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,N}\rangle+f_{2,N}(\xi)+\mathrm{i}\langle\xi,\,T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\rangle-f_{2}(\xi)\bigr)\big|

\displaystyle\,=0\,.\qed

5.2.  Brownian paths

We recall now some basic notions for Brownian paths.
For
0\leqslant\tilde{\tau}<\tau
, let
\Omega^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}
be the space of continuous paths
\omega\vcentcolon[\tilde{\tau},\tau]\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and for
x,\tilde{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\Omega^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}_{x,\tilde{x}}
the space of continuous paths that start at
\tilde{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
at time
\tilde{\tau}
and finish at
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
at time
\tau
. Given
x,\tilde{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
0\leqslant\tilde{\tau}<\tau
, we denote by
\mathbb{P}_{x,\tilde{x}}^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}
the law of the Brownian bridge equal to
\tilde{x}
at time
\tilde{\tau}
and to
x
at time
\tau
.
Moreover, for
x,\tilde{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
0\leqslant\tilde{\tau}<\tau
, we define the positive measure over paths in
\Omega^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}
through

\mathbb{W}_{x,\tilde{x}}^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\psi^{\tau-\tilde{\tau}}(x-\tilde{x})\mathbb{P}_{x,\tilde{x}}^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\,,

(5.31)

where for
t>0
we defined the heat kernel

\psi^{t}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{t\Delta/2}\bigr)_{x,0}=(2\pi t)^{-d/2}\mathrm{e}^{-|x|^{2}/2t}\,.

(5.32)

The law
\mathbb{W}_{x,\tilde{x}}^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}
is characterised by its finite dimensional distribution. For
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,
\tilde{\tau}<t_{1}<\cdots<t_{n}<\tau
and
f\vcentcolon\mathbb{R}^{dn}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
a continuous function, we have

\displaystyle\int\mathbb{W}

\displaystyle{}_{\tilde{x},x}^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\,f\bigl(\omega(t_{1}),\ldots,\omega(t_{n})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}x_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}x_{n}\,\psi^{t_{1}-\tilde{\tau}}(x_{1}-\tilde{x})\psi^{t_{2}-t_{1}}(x_{2}-x_{1})\cdots\psi^{t_{n}-t_{n-1}}(x_{n}-x_{n-1})

\displaystyle\times\psi^{\tau-t_{n}}(x-x_{n})f(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\,.

In the next lemma we provide some useful bounds, whose proofs can be found in
7
;
6
.

Lemma 5.10

(Analogous to
(
6
, Lemmas 2.2 and 2.3)
)
.

(i)

There exists a constant
C\equiv C_{d}>0
such that, for all
0\leqslant\tilde{\tau}<\tau
, we have

\sup_{x,\tilde{x}}\int\mathbb{W}^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}_{x,\tilde{x}}(\mathrm{d}\omega)=\sup_{x,\tilde{x}}\psi^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}(x-\tilde{x})\leqslant\frac{C}{(\tau-\tilde{\tau})^{\frac{d}{2}}}\,.

(ii)

There exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
\tilde{\tau}\leqslant s\leqslant t\leqslant\tau
, we have

\int\mathbb{P}_{x,\tilde{x}}^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\,|\omega(t)-\omega(s)|^{2}\leqslant C\biggl((t-s)+|x-\tilde{x}|^{2}\frac{(t-s)^{2}}{(\tau-\tilde{\tau})^{2}}\biggr)\,.

The following result is well known.

Lemma 5.11

(Feynman-Kac formula)
.

Let
I\in\mathbb{R}
be a closed bounded interval and
V\vcentcolon I\times{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\to[0,\infty)
a locally bounded function. Let
\bigl(W^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}\bigr)_{\tilde{\tau}\leqslant\tau\in I}
be the propagator satisfying

\partial_{\tau}W^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}=\bigg(\frac{\Delta}{2}+V(\tau)\biggr)W^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}\quad W^{\tau,\tau}=1\,.

Then
W^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}
has operator kernel

W_{x,\tilde{x}}^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}=\int\mathbb{W}_{x,\tilde{x}}^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\,\mathrm{e}^{\int_{\tilde{\tau}}^{\tau}\mathrm{d}s\,V(s,\omega(s))}\,.

5.3.  Proof of Proposition
5.6

In this subsection we show Proposition
5.6
. In particular we explain how to adapt the strategy in
(
6
, Proposition 3.12)
to our setting. To keep the paper as concise as possible, we present here only the main changes.

Proof of Proposition
5.6
.

Consider
H^{0}
as in (
2.13
) and
H_{n}
the restriction of the Hamiltonian (
2.18
) to the
n
-particle space
\mathcal{H}_{n}
.
Let us define on the Fock space
\mathcal{F}
the operator
R_{\eta}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\bigoplus_{n\geqslant 0}R_{n,\eta}
for
\eta>0
, where

(R_{n,\eta})_{{\mathbf{x}},\mathbf{\tilde{x}}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\mathrm{e}^{-\nu\kappa n+E^{\varepsilon}}\int\mu_{C_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\sigma)\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\langle\sigma,\varrho^{U}_{\nu}\rangle_{\nu}-\langle\tau^{\varepsilon},\varrho^{U}_{\nu}\rangle}\\
\times\int\biggl(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{W}_{x_{i},\tilde{x}_{i}}^{\nu,0}(\mathrm{d}\omega_{i})\mathrm{e}^{-{\int\limits_{0}^{\nu}\mathrm{d}t\,(U(\omega_{i}(t))-\tau^{\varepsilon}(\omega_{i}(t)))}}\biggr)\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\int\limits_{0}^{\nu}\mathrm{d}t\,\sigma(t,\omega_{i}(t))},

(5.33)

and

\langle\sigma,\varrho\rangle_{\nu}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\frac{1}{\nu}\int_{0}^{\nu}\mathrm{d}\tau\int\mathrm{d}x\,\sigma(\tau,x)\varrho(x)\,.

From
(
6
, Proposition 3.12)
, the operator kernel (
5.33
) converges locally uniformly to
\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-H_{n}}\bigr)_{{\mathbf{x}},\mathbf{\tilde{x}}}
.

The rest of the proof is devoted to showing that the regularised partition function

\mathcal{Z}_{\eta}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\Tr_{\mathcal{F}}(R_{\eta})/\Tr_{\mathcal{F}}\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-H^{0}}\bigr)\,.

can be written as the right-hand side of (
5.11
).
We start by replacing
\varrho_{\nu}^{U}
by
\varrho_{\nu}^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}
when convenient, by adding the term (
5.14
). We obtain

\eqref{eq:R_phi42}=\mathrm{e}^{-\nu\kappa n+E^{\varepsilon}}\int\mu_{C_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\sigma)\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\langle\sigma,\varrho^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}_{\nu}\rangle_{\nu}-\mathrm{i}\langle\sigma,T^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}\rangle_{\nu}-\langle\tau^{\varepsilon},\varrho^{U}_{\nu}\rangle}\\
\times\int\biggl(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{W}_{x_{i},\tilde{x}_{i}}^{\nu,0}(\mathrm{d}\omega_{i})\mathrm{e}^{-{\int\limits_{0}^{\nu}\mathrm{d}t\,(U(\omega_{i}(t))-\tau^{\varepsilon}(\omega_{i}(t)))}}\biggr)\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\int\limits_{0}^{\nu}\mathrm{d}t\,\sigma(t,\omega_{i}(t))}\,.

(5.34)

We introduce the function
\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}
in (
5.34
) by performing a Hubbard-Stratonovich transformation similarly as in
6
, with an additional term
\alpha^{\varepsilon}
, and then compensate for the resulting contributions. In particular, for
f(t,x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta(x-\omega_{i}(t))-\varrho^{U}_{\nu}(x)/\nu-\alpha^{\varepsilon}(x)/\nu
, we find that

\begin{split}-\frac{1}{2}\langle f,C_{\eta}f\rangle&=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\nu}\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{t}\int\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta(x-\omega_{i}(t))-\frac{\varrho_{\nu}(x)}{\nu}\biggr)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\times\bigl(\nu\delta_{\eta,\nu}(t-\tilde{t})v^{\varepsilon}(x-\tilde{x})\varphi(\eta x)\varphi(\eta\tilde{x})\bigr)\biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta(\tilde{x}-\omega_{i}(\tilde{t}))-\frac{\varrho_{\nu}(\tilde{x})}{\nu}\biggr)\\
&+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{\nu}\mathrm{d}t\,\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(\omega_{i}(t))-\langle\alpha^{\varepsilon},\varrho^{U}_{\nu}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\,\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)\alpha^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x})\,.\end{split}

Hence, we can rewrite

\eqref{eq:R_phi42, second exp}=\mathrm{e}^{-\nu\kappa n+E^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\,\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)\alpha^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x})}\int\mu_{C_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\sigma)\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\langle\sigma,\varrho^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}_{\nu}\rangle_{\nu}-\mathrm{i}\langle\sigma,T^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}+\alpha^{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\nu}+\langle\alpha^{\varepsilon}-\tau^{\varepsilon},\varrho^{U}_{\nu}\rangle}\\
\times\int\biggl(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{W}_{x_{i},\tilde{x}_{i}}^{\nu,0}(\mathrm{d}\omega_{i})\mathrm{e}^{-{\int\limits_{0}^{\nu}\mathrm{d}t\,U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(\omega_{i}(t))}}\biggr)\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\int\limits_{0}^{\nu}\mathrm{d}t\,\sigma(t,\omega_{i}(t))}\,.

(5.35)

Taking the trace of
R_{\eta}
we find

\Tr_{\mathcal{F}}(R_{\eta})=\mathrm{e}^{E^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\,\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)\alpha^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{x})}\int\mu_{C_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\sigma)\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\langle\sigma,T^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}+\alpha^{\varepsilon}\rangle_{\nu}+\langle\alpha^{\varepsilon}-\tau^{\varepsilon},\varrho^{U}_{\nu}\rangle}\\
\times\exp\biggl\{\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\Tr\biggl(\frac{1}{t+K(-\kappa-U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\mathrm{i}\sigma)}-\frac{1}{t+K(-\kappa-U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta})}\mathrm{i}\sigma\frac{1}{t+K(-\kappa-U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta})}\biggr)\biggr\}

where we used the fact that from
(
6
, lemma 3.13)

\big\langle\sigma,\varrho_{\nu}^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}\big\rangle_{\nu}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\Tr\biggl(\frac{1}{t+K(-\kappa-U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta})}\sigma\frac{1}{t+K(-\kappa-U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta})}\biggr)\,.

Finally, recalling (
5.13
) and

\Tr_{\mathcal{F}}\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-H^{0}}\bigr)=\exp\biggl\{\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\Tr\biggl(\frac{1}{t+K(-\kappa-U)}\biggr)\biggr\}\,,

we can conclude the proof.
∎

6. Proof of Proposition
3.1

In this section we show Proposition
3.1
. We use the functional integral representation introduced in Section
5
to study the rate of convergence of the relative partition function and the correlation functions in the mean-field limit, while keeping track of the parameter
\varepsilon
. The basic strategy is similar to that of
(
7
, Section 5)
. However, as we saw in Section
5
, in the inhomogeneous setting the functional integral representation is more complicated and the approach has to be suitably adapted. Our methods work for
d=2,3
, and hence all results of this section are stated for both dimensions.

Furthermore, as a result of our analysis, in analogy with
7
, we have some restrictions on the choice of the interaction potential
v^{\varepsilon}
. In particular, we have that
\varepsilon\equiv\varepsilon(\nu)>0
satisfies the lower bounds in (
3.3
).
Let us define
\chi\vcentcolon[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
by

\chi(t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\begin{cases}\log{t^{-1}}&d=2\\
t^{-1}&d=3\,.\end{cases}

(6.1)

From Lemmas
7.6
and
5.1
, we have, uniformly in
\eta>0
, that

\|\tau^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}\vee\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim_{v,\kappa}\chi(\varepsilon)

as well as the bounds

E^{\varepsilon}\vee S^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\lesssim_{v}\chi(\varepsilon)^{2}\,.

(6.2)

Moreover, (
3.3
) and (
6.1
) imply that, for all
C,b>0

\lim_{\nu\rightarrow 0}\mathrm{e}^{C\chi(\varepsilon)^{2}}\nu^{b}=0\,.

(6.3)

The following propositions and lemma, which contains quantitative estimates on the relative partition function and the correlation functions, together with (
6.3
), allow us to show Proposition
3.1
.

Proposition 6.1
.

There exists
C\equiv C_{\kappa,v}>0
such that

\lvert\mathcal{Z}-\zeta^{W^{\varepsilon}}\rvert\lesssim_{\kappa,v,s,\theta}\mathrm{e}^{C\chi(\varepsilon)^{2}}(\nu^{1/4}\vee\nu^{1-s/2})

for any
s\in(d/2,2)
satisfying (
2.3
).

Lemma 6.2
.

There exists a constant
C\equiv C_{\kappa,v}
for all
p\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

(\widehat{\gamma}_{p})_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}\vee\nu^{p}(\widehat{\Gamma}_{p})_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}\lesssim_{d,v,\kappa,p,\theta}\mathrm{e}^{C\chi(\varepsilon)^{2}}\Upsilon_{\theta}({\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}})\,.

The result follows from
(
6
, Proposition 7.2)
, recalling Propositions
5.3
and
5.6
and (
6.2
).

Using a more precise bound, we improve
(
7
, Proposition 5.2)
and obtain the following analogous result regarding the convergence of the Wick-ordered correlation function.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
s\in(d/2,2)
satisfy (
2.3
).
Consider
R>0
and let
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{\tilde{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{dp}
satisfy
\lvert\mathbf{\tilde{x}}\rvert+\lvert\mathbf{x}\rvert\leqslant R
. We define

\theta(s,d)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\frac{(2-s)(4-d)}{8}\wedge\frac{(4-d)}{16}\,.

Then, for all
p\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a constant
C_{1}
depending on
v,\kappa
such that

\big|{\nu^{p}(\widehat{\Gamma}_{p})_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}-(\widehat{\gamma}_{p})_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}}\big|\lesssim_{d,p,v,\kappa}\mathrm{e}^{C_{1}\chi(\varepsilon)^{2}}\nu^{\theta(s,d)}(1+\sqrt{\nu R})\,.

Proof.

The proof follows from
(
7
, Section 5.2,Appendix C)
, using Proposition
6.1
and the second estimate of Lemma
5.10
; we omit further details.
∎

We can now show Proposition
3.1
.

Proof of Proposition
3.1
.

We obtain the convergence of the relative partition functions as a direct consequence of Proposition
6.1
. The bound in (
3.4
) follows from

\lim_{\varepsilon,\nu\to 0}\sup_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}\frac{\big|\nu^{p}(\widehat{\Gamma}_{p})_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}-(\widehat{\gamma}_{p}^{W^{\varepsilon}})_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}\big|}{\Upsilon_{\theta-c}(\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}})}=0\,.

(6.4)

Hence, we show (
6.4
).
Let
c,R>0
, we have from Lemma
6.2

\sup_{\lvert\mathbf{x}\rvert+\lvert\mathbf{\tilde{x}}\rvert>R}\frac{\big|{\nu^{p}(\widehat{\Gamma}_{p})_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}-(\widehat{\gamma}_{p})_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}}\big|}{\Upsilon_{\theta-c}({\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}})}\leqslant\sup_{\lvert\mathbf{x}\rvert+\lvert\mathbf{\tilde{x}}\rvert>R}\frac{C\mathrm{e}^{C_{1}\chi(\varepsilon)^{2}}\Upsilon_{\theta}({\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}})}{\Upsilon_{\theta-c}({\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}})}\leqslant C\mathrm{e}^{C_{1}\chi(\varepsilon)^{2}}R^{-c(2-d/2)}\,.

(6.5)

By Proposition
6.3
,

\sup_{\lvert\mathbf{x}\rvert+\lvert\mathbf{\tilde{x}}\rvert\leqslant R}\frac{\big|{\nu^{p}(\widehat{\Gamma}_{p})_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}-(\widehat{\gamma}_{p})_{\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}}}\big|}{\Upsilon_{\theta-c}({\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{x}}})}\leqslant C\mathrm{e}^{C_{1}\chi(\varepsilon)^{2}}\nu^{\theta(s,d)}(1+\sqrt{\nu R})R^{\theta(2-d/2)}\,.

Choosing
R=\nu^{-\alpha}
with
\alpha>0
small enough (depending on
d,s
), we can conclude the proof.
∎

6.1.  Proof of Proposition
6.1

The rest of the section is dedicated to show Proposition
6.1
. We start by noticing the following result, whose proof is similar to the corresponding lemma in
(
7
, Lemma 5.9)
.

Lemma 6.4
.

Recall (
5.8
), (
5.9
), and (
5.10
) as well as (
5.12
), (
5.13
), and (
5.14
).
Uniformly in
\eta\geqslant 0
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\big|\mathcal{Z}_{\eta}-\zeta^{W^{\varepsilon}}\big|\leqslant\mathrm{e}^{C\chi(\varepsilon)^{2}}\biggl[\biggl(\int\mu_{\mathcal{C}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\sigma)\big|F_{2}(\sigma)-f_{2}(\langle\sigma\rangle)\big|^{2}\biggr)^{1/2}+\lvert S^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}-S^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\rvert\\
+\int\mu_{v^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\xi)\big|\langle\xi,T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-T^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}\rangle\big|\biggr]\,.

(6.6)

In order to show Proposition
6.1
, we need some key bounds on the three sums on the right-hand side of (
6.6
). We are going to split the analysis in two different steps. First, we need to control the terms containing the differences
S^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}-S^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}
and
T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-T^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}
. Then, we consider the term with difference of the functions
F_{2}(\sigma)-f_{2}(\langle\sigma\rangle)
. We sum up the results in the following two lemmas. Hence, the proof of Proposition
6.1
is a direct consequence of Lemma
6.4
together with Lemma
6.5
and
6.6
below.

Lemma 6.5
.

Uniformly in
\eta\geqslant 0
small enough, and for any
s\in(d/2,2)
satisfying (
2.3
)

\big|S^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}-S^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\big|+\int\mu_{v^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\xi)\big|\langle\xi,T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-T^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}\rangle\big|\lesssim_{\kappa,s}\chi(\varepsilon)^{2}\nu^{1/2-s/4}\,.

Lemma 6.6
.

Uniformly in
\eta\geqslant 0
small enough, and for any
s\in(d/2,2)
satisfying (
2.3
)

\int\mu_{\mathcal{C}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\sigma)|F_{2}(\sigma)-f_{2}(\langle\sigma\rangle)|^{2}\lesssim_{\kappa,v,s}\frac{\nu^{1/2}\vee\nu^{2-s}}{\varepsilon^{5d+1}}\,.

6.1.1. Proof of Lemma
6.5

We split the proof of Lemma
6.5
in two steps. We start with the bound of the term involving the functions
T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}
and
T^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}
defined in (
5.10
) and (
5.14
) respectively.

Lemma 6.7
.

Let the
T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}
and
T^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}
be as in (
5.10
) and (
5.14
). Let
\xi
be a centred Gaussian field of covariance
v^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}
. Then, under the assumptions of Lemma
6.5
,

\int\mu_{v^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\xi)\big|\langle\xi,T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-T^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}\rangle\big|\lesssim_{s,\kappa}\nu^{1/2-s/4}\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}\,.

(6.7)

Proof.

Note that

\varrho_{\nu}(x)=\Bigl(\frac{\nu}{\mathrm{e}^{\nu h}-1}\Bigr)(x,x)=\Bigl(\frac{\nu\mathrm{e}^{-\nu h}}{1-\mathrm{e}^{-\nu h}}\Bigr)(x,x)=\nu\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-\nu nh}\bigr)(x,x)\,.

Thus from the definition (
5.14
), and Feynman-Kac formula, we can rewrite

\displaystyle T^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}(x)

\displaystyle=\nu\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-\nu nh^{U}}-\mathrm{e}^{-\nu nh^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}}\bigr)(x,x)

\displaystyle=\nu\sum_{t\in\nu\mathbb{N}^{*}}\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa t}\int\mathbb{W}^{t,0}_{x,x}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(\omega(s))}-\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(\omega(s))}\bigr)\,.

(6.8)

Using the assumptions that
U,U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}>0
, we find a uniform upper bound on
T^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}
in both
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\eta>0
small enough, since

\displaystyle\lvert T^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}(x)\rvert

\displaystyle\leqslant\nu\sum_{t\in\nu\mathbb{N}^{*}}\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa t}\int\mathbb{W}^{t,0}_{x,x}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\,\Big|\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(\omega(s))}-\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(\omega(s))}\Big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}\nu\sum_{t\in\nu\mathbb{N}^{*}}t\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa t}\int\mathbb{W}^{t,0}_{x,x}(\mathrm{d}\omega)

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{d/2-2}\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}\,.

(6.9)

for every
\varepsilon>0
, where we used a Riemann sum estimate in the last line together with Lemma
5.10
(i). Proceeding as in (
5.30
), we find

\biggl(\int\mu_{v^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\xi)\lvert\langle\xi,T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-T^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}\rangle\rvert\biggr)^{2}\lesssim\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-T^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}\|_{L^{1}}

(6.10)

which follows from (
6.9
) and Lemma
5.9

(ii)
. Hence, (
6.7
) follows if we can show

\|T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-T^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim_{s}\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}\nu^{1-\frac{s}{2}}\,.

(6.11)

By duality, we have

\|T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-T^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}\|_{L^{1}}=\sup_{\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant 1}\biggl\lvert\int\mathrm{d}x\,(T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-T^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta})(x)\varphi(x)\biggr\rvert\,.

For a fixed
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}
with
\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\leqslant 1
, we consider the expression inside the integral

T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(x)-T^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}(x)=\int\mathrm{d}t\,\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-th^{U}}-\mathrm{e}^{-th^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}}\bigr)(x,x)-\nu\sum_{t\in\nu\mathbb{N}}\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-th^{U}}-\mathrm{e}^{-th^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}}\bigr)(x,x)\,.

(6.12)

Thus, to bound
\lvert\int\mathrm{d}x\,(T^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-T^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta})(x)\varphi(x)\rvert
, we can estimate the two terms on the right-hand side of (
6.12
) considering small and large times. For small times
t\leqslant\delta
we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\int\mathrm{d}x\,\nu\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\in\nu\mathbb{N}\\
t\leqslant\delta\end{subarray}}\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-th^{U}}-\mathrm{e}^{-th^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}}\bigr)(x,x)\varphi(x)\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s}\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}\delta^{2-s}

(6.13)

and a similar estimate can be obtained if we replace the Riemann sum by the corresponding integral. For large times
t>\delta
, we compare the differences between the Riemann sums and the corresponding integral and find

\displaystyle\lvert\int\mathrm{d}x\int_{\delta}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-th^{U}}-\mathrm{e}^{-[t]_{\nu}h^{U}}\bigr)(x,x)\varphi(x)\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s}\nu\delta^{-s}

(6.14)

where we used the notation
[t]_{\nu}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\min\{s\in\nu\mathbb{N^{*}}\vcentcolon s<t\}
. The same bound holds if we replace
h^{U}
in (
6.14
) by
h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}
. Optimizing over the size
\delta\sim\nu^{1/2}
yields the claim (
6.11
).
To conclude the proof of (
6.7
), it remains to show (
6.13
) and (
6.14
). We start with (
6.13
), using Duhamel’s formula and (
5.5
), we have

\displaystyle\Biggr|\int\mathrm{d}x\,

\displaystyle\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-th^{U}}-\mathrm{e}^{-th^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}}\bigr)(x,x)\varphi(x)\Biggl|

(6.15)

\displaystyle=\lvert\int\mathrm{d}x\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\lambda\,\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda th^{U}}t(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})\mathrm{e}^{-(1-\lambda)th^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}}\bigr)(x,x)\varphi(x)\rvert

\displaystyle\leqslant t\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\lambda\int\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda th^{U/2}}(x,\tilde{x})\mathrm{e}^{-(1-\lambda)th^{U/2}}(\tilde{x},x)

\displaystyle\leqslant t\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\tr\bigl({\mathrm{e}^{-th^{U/2}}\bigr)}\,.

(6.16)

Thus, using the bound

\tr{\mathrm{e}^{-th^{U/2}}}\lesssim_{s}t^{-s}\tr{h^{-s}}\lesssim_{s}t^{-s},

and (
6.16
) we finally have that the left-hand side of (
6.13
) is bounded by

\eqref{eq:Tsmalltime}\leqslant C_{s}\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}\nu\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\in\nu\mathbb{N}\\
t\leqslant\delta\end{subarray}}t^{1-s}\lesssim_{s}\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}\delta^{2-s}\,,

where in the last step we performed a Riemann sum approximation.

As for (
6.14
), using Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\lvert\int\mathrm{d}x\,\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-th^{U}}-\mathrm{e}^{-[t]_{\nu}h^{U}}\bigr)(x,x)\varphi(x)\rvert

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{[t]_{\nu}}^{t}\mathrm{d}r\,\lvert\tr\bigl({h\mathrm{e}^{-rh^{U}}\varphi}\bigr)\rvert

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{[t]_{\nu}}^{t}\mathrm{d}r\,\tr\bigl({h\mathrm{e}^{-rh}\bigr)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s}\int_{[t]_{\nu}}^{t}\mathrm{d}r\,\tr\Bigl({h\frac{1}{h^{1+s}}}\Bigr)\frac{1}{r^{1+s}}\lesssim_{s}\nu t^{-1-s}\,,

(6.17)

(
6.14
) then follows by integrating (
6.17
) with respect to
t\in(\delta,\infty)
.
∎

We now prove the estimate for the term involving the constants
S_{\eta}^{\varepsilon}
and
S^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}
defined in (
5.9
) and (
5.13
) respectively.

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
S_{\eta}^{\varepsilon}
and
S^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}
be as in (
5.9
) and (
5.13
). Then, under the assumptions of Lemma
6.5

\lvert S^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-S^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}\rvert\lesssim_{\kappa,s}\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\nu^{2-s}\,.

(6.18)

Proof.

From (
5.20
), we can write

\displaystyle S_{\eta}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\tr\Bigl({\frac{1}{t+h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}}-\frac{1}{t+h^{U}}+\frac{1}{t+h^{U}}(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})\frac{1}{t+h^{U}}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\tr{\Bigl(\frac{1}{t+h^{U}}(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})\frac{1}{t+h^{U}}(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})\frac{1}{t+h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}}}\Bigr)}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\int_{[0,\infty)^{3}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{r}\,\tr{\Bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-r_{1}(t+h^{U})}(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})\mathrm{e}^{-r_{2}(t+h^{U})}(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})\mathrm{e}^{-r_{3}(t+h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}})}\Bigr)}

(6.19)

where
\lvert\mathbf{r}\rvert=r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3}
. From
(
6
, proof of lemma 5.4)
with a re-indexing of the variables
\tau_{i},i=1,2,3
, we can write
S^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}
similarly as

S^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\int_{[0,\nu]^{3}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{\tau}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{q}\in(\nu\mathbb{N})^{3}\\
\lvert\mathbf{q}\rvert>0\end{subarray}}\tr\Bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-(q_{1}+\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})(t+h^{U})}(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})\mathrm{e}^{-(q_{2}+\tau_{2}-\tau_{3})(t+h^{U})}\\
\times(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})\mathrm{e}^{-(q_{3}+\tau_{3}-\tau_{1})(t+h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}})}\Bigr)\,.

(6.20)

By performing the change of variables
r_{i}=q_{i}+\tau_{i}
and denoting
\{r_{i}\}_{\nu}=r_{i}-[r_{i}]_{\nu}\in[0,\nu)
, we can rewrite (
6.20
) as

S^{\varepsilon}_{\nu,\eta}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\int_{[0,\infty)^{3}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{r}\,\mathbbold{1}_{\lvert r\rvert\geqslant\nu}\tr\Bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-(r_{1}-\{r_{2}\}_{\nu})(t+h^{U})}(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})\mathrm{e}^{-(r_{2}-\{r_{3}\}_{\nu})(t+h^{U})}\\
\times(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})\mathrm{e}^{-(r_{3}-\{r_{1}\}_{\nu})(t+h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}})}\Bigr)\,.

(6.21)

We proceed as in the proof of Lemma
6.7
splitting the estimate in small and large times: first, we restrict the integration to the case where one of the time variables
r_{i}
lies in the interval
[0,\delta]
, where
\delta\equiv\delta(\nu)=C_{0}\nu
and
C_{0}\geqslant 2
is a positive constant. Then we consider when the time variables
r_{i}>\delta
. In the first case we have the bounds for
S_{\eta}^{\varepsilon}

\biggl\lvert\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\int_{[0,\infty)^{3}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{r}\,\mathbbold{1}_{\min_{i}{r_{i}}\leqslant\delta}\,\tr\Bigl({\mathrm{e}^{-r_{1}(t+h^{U})}(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})\mathrm{e}^{-r_{2}(t+h^{U})}(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})\mathrm{e}^{-r_{3}(t+h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}})}}\Bigr)\biggr\rvert\\
\lesssim_{s}\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\delta^{2-s}\,,

(6.22)

and for
S_{\nu,\eta}^{\varepsilon}

\Biggl|\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\int_{[0,\infty)^{3}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{r}\,\mathbbold{1}_{\nu\leqslant\min_{i}{r_{i}}\leqslant\delta}\\
\times\tr\Bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-(r_{1}-\{r_{2}\}_{\nu})(t+h^{U})}(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})\mathrm{e}^{-(r_{2}-\{r_{3}\}_{\nu})(t+h^{U})}(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})\mathrm{e}^{-(r_{3}-\{r_{1}\}_{\nu})(t+h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}})}\Bigr)\Biggr|\\
\lesssim_{s}\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\delta^{2-s}\,.

(6.23)

On the other hand, for large times, we have

\Biggl|\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\int_{\mathbf{r}\in[\delta,\infty)^{3}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{r}\,\tr\Bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-(r_{1}-\{r_{2}\}_{\nu})(t+h^{U})}(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})\mathrm{e}^{-(r_{2}-\{r_{3}\}_{\nu})(t+h^{U})}(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})\\
\times\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-r_{3}(t+h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}})}-\mathrm{e}^{-(r_{3}-\{r_{1}\}_{\nu})(t+h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}})}\bigr)\Bigr)\Biggr|\lesssim_{s}\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\nu(\delta-\nu)^{1-s}\,.

(6.24)

Hence, the proof of (
6.18
) follows from (
6.22
), (
6.23
) and by using a telescoping argument, applying (
6.24
) to each pair of terms.

Now we need to establish (
6.22
), (
6.23
) and (
6.24
). For (
6.22
), it is sufficient to show

\biggl\lvert\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\int_{[0,\infty)^{3}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{r}\,\mathbbold{1}_{{r_{3}}\leqslant\delta}\tr{\mathrm{e}^{-r_{1}(t+h^{U})}(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})\mathrm{e}^{-r_{2}(t+h^{U})}(\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon})\mathrm{e}^{-r_{3}(t+h^{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}})}}\biggr\rvert\\
\lesssim_{s}\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\delta^{2-s}\,.

(6.25)

From the bound in (
5.5
), we find that the left-hand side of (
6.25
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\int_{[0,\infty)^{3}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{r}\,\mathbbold{1}_{{r_{3}}\leqslant\delta}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\int\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\,\mathrm{e}^{-r_{1}(t+h^{U/2})}(x_{1},x_{2})\mathrm{e}^{-r_{2}(t+h^{U/2})}(x_{2},x_{3})\mathrm{e}^{-r_{3}(t+h^{U/2})}(x_{3},x_{1})\\
&\quad\leqslant\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\int_{[0,\infty)^{3}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{r}\,\mathbbold{1}_{{r_{3}}\leqslant\delta}\tr{\mathrm{e}^{-r_{1}(t+h^{U/2})}\mathrm{e}^{-r_{2}(t+h^{U/2})}\mathrm{e}^{-r_{3}(t+h^{{U/2}})}}\\
&\quad=\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\tr\Bigl({\frac{1}{(t+h^{U/2})^{3}}(1-\mathrm{e}^{-\delta(t+h^{U/2})})}\Bigr)\,.\end{split}

(6.26)

For fixed
\lambda>0
, using the fact that
h^{U/2}
is a positive operator and the Hölder’s inequality for Schatten norms, we find

\begin{split}\tr\biggl(&\frac{1}{(t+h^{U/2})^{3}}(1-\mathrm{e}^{-\delta(t+h^{U/2})})\biggr)\\
&=\tr\biggl(\frac{1}{(t+h^{U/2})^{3}}(1-\mathrm{e}^{-\delta(t+h^{U/2})})(\mathbbold{1}_{t+h^{U/2}\leqslant\lambda}+\mathbbold{1}_{h^{U/2}+t>\lambda})\biggr)\\
&\leqslant\delta\tr\biggl({\frac{1}{(t+h^{U/2})^{2}}\mathbbold{1}_{t+h^{U/2}\leqslant\lambda}}\biggr)+\tr\biggl({\frac{1}{(t+h^{U/2})^{3}}\mathbbold{1}_{h^{U/2}+t>\lambda}}\biggr)\\
&\leqslant\delta\tr\biggl({\frac{1}{(t+h^{U/2})^{1+s}}(t+h^{U/2})^{s-1}\mathbbold{1}_{t+h^{U/2}\leqslant\lambda}}\biggr)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\tr\biggl({\frac{1}{(t+h^{U/2})^{1+s}}(t+h^{U/2})^{s-2}\mathbbold{1}_{h^{U/2}+t>\lambda}}\biggr)\\
&\leqslant\tr\biggl({\frac{1}{(t+h^{U/2})^{1+s}}}\biggr)(\delta\lambda^{s-1}+\lambda^{s-2})\,.\end{split}

(6.27)

Choosing
\lambda=\delta^{-1}
in (
6.27
), we get

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:small time estimate S_eta_reduced claim bound before t integration}\leqslant\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\delta^{2-s}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\tr\biggl({\frac{1}{(t+h^{U/2})^{1+s}}}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\delta^{2-s}\tr\biggl({\frac{1}{(h^{U/2})^{s}}\biggr)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s}\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\delta^{2-s}\,,

which proves (
6.25
).

In a similar way, we can establish (
6.23
) by showing the bound

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\int_{[0,\infty)^{3}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{r}\,\mathbbold{1}_{\lvert r\rvert\geqslant\nu}\mathbbold{1}_{r_{3}\leqslant\delta}\\
&\hskip 99.58464pt\times\tr\Bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-(r_{1}-\{r_{2}\}_{\nu})(t+h^{U/2})}\mathrm{e}^{-(r_{2}-\{r_{3}\}_{\nu})(t+h^{U/2})}\mathrm{e}^{-(r_{3}-\{r_{1}\}_{\nu})(t+h^{U/2})}\Bigr)\\
&\quad=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\nu^{3}\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{\nu\mathbb{N}}^{3}}\mathbbold{1}_{\lvert r\rvert\geqslant\nu}\mathbbold{1}_{r_{3}\leqslant\delta}\tr\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-\lvert\mathbf{r}\rvert(t+h^{U/2})}\bigr)\\
&\quad\leqslant\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\nu^{3}\sum_{r_{3}\in{\nu\mathbb{N}^{*}}}\tr\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-\lvert r_{3}\rvert(t+h^{U/2})}\bigr)+\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\nu^{3}\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in{\nu\mathbb{N}}^{3}}\mathbbold{1}_{r_{3}\leqslant\delta}\mathbbold{1}_{r_{1}+r_{2}\geqslant\nu}\tr\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-\lvert\mathbf{r}\rvert(t+h^{U/2})}\bigr)\\
&\quad\lesssim C_{s}\nu^{3}\sum_{r_{3}\in{\nu\mathbb{N}^{*}}}\frac{1}{r_{3}^{1+s}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\tr\biggl(\frac{1}{(t+h^{U/2})^{1+s}}\biggr)\\
&\quad\quad+C_{s}\delta\nu^{2}\sum_{r_{1},r_{2}\in{\nu\mathbb{N}^{*}}}\frac{1}{{(r_{1}+r_{2})}^{1+s}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\tr\biggl(\frac{1}{(t+h^{U/2})^{1+s}}\biggr)\lesssim_{s}\nu^{2-s}+\delta\nu^{1-s}\lesssim_{s}\delta^{2-s}\,,\end{split}

(6.28)

where in the last step we used that
\tr\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-r_{3}h}\bigr)\leqslant C_{s}\tr\Bigl(\frac{1}{(r_{3}h)^{1+s}}\Bigr)
to bound the two terms.
To conclude, we need to show (
6.24
). Using Hölder’s inequality, the fact that, for a multiplication operator
f
, we have
9
9

9

We use
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\mathfrak{S}^{p}}
to denote the
p
-th Schatten norm.

\|f\|_{\mathfrak{S^{\infty}}}=\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}
, and for
h>0
we have
\int_{s}^{t}\mathrm{d}\lambda\,h\,\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda h}=\mathrm{e}^{-sh}-\mathrm{e}^{-th}
, we can bound the left-hand side of (
6.24
) by

\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\int_{[\delta,\infty)^{3}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{r}\,\mathrm{e}^{-{(|\mathbf{r}|-3\nu)\kappa}}\\
\times\big\|\mathrm{e}^{-(r_{1}-\{r_{2}\}_{\nu})(t-\Delta/2+U)}\big\|_{\mathfrak{S}^{3}}\big\|\mathrm{e}^{-(r_{2}-\{r_{3}\}_{\nu})(t-\Delta/2+U)}\big\|_{\mathfrak{S}^{3}}\\
\times\int_{0}^{\{r_{1}\}_{\nu}}\mathrm{d}\lambda\,\big\|(t-\Delta/2+{U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}})\mathrm{e}^{-(r_{3}-\lambda)(t-\Delta/2+U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta})}\big\|_{\mathfrak{S}^{3}}\,.

(6.29)

Since
\mathrm{e}^{-3x}x^{3}\leqslant\mathrm{e}^{-x}
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
, we find

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{\{r_{1}\}_{\nu}}\mathrm{d}\lambda\,\big\|(t-\Delta/2+U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta})\mathrm{e}^{-(r_{3}-\lambda)(t-\Delta/2+U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta})}\big\|_{\mathfrak{S}^{3}}\\
&\quad\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\int_{0}^{\{r_{1}\}_{\nu}}\mathrm{d}\lambda\,\tr\Bigl({\bigl(t-\Delta/2+U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}\bigr)^{3}\mathrm{e}^{-3(r_{3}-\lambda)(t-\Delta/2+U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta})}}\Bigr)^{1/3}\\
&\quad\leqslant\nu\frac{1}{r_{3}-\nu}\tr{\Bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-(r_{3}-\nu)(t-\Delta/2+U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta})}\Bigr)}^{1/3}\,.\end{split}

(6.30)

Moreover, using the bound

\tr{\Bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-(r_{i}-\nu)(t-\Delta/2+U)}\Bigr)}^{1/3}\vee\tr{\Bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-(r_{i}-\nu)(t-\Delta/2+U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta})}\Bigr)}^{1/3}\lesssim_{a}\frac{1}{(r_{i}-\nu)^{a}}\tr\biggl({\frac{1}{(t-\Delta/2+U/2)^{3a}}}\biggr)^{1/3}

with
a=\frac{s+1}{3}
, together with (
6.30
) we can conclude

(
6.29
)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{s}\nu\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\int_{\mathbf{r}\in[\delta-\nu,\infty)^{3}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{r}\,\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\lvert\mathbf{r}\rvert\kappa}}{r_{1}^{a}r_{2}^{a}r_{3}^{1+a}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\tr{\frac{1}{(t-\Delta/2+U/2)^{3a}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\kappa,s}\|\alpha^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}-\tau^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\nu(\delta-\nu)^{1-s}\,.\qed

6.1.2. Proof of Lemma
6.6

We finally prove Lemma
6.6
. We proceed by introducing two lemmas which are similar to
(
7
, Lemma 5.10-5.11)
. The two results below show some quantitative estimates for the quantities
F_{2}(\sigma)
and
f_{2}(\xi)
as defined in (
5.8
) and (
5.12
).

Lemma 6.9
.

Uniformly in
\eta>0
, we have

\bigg|\int\mu_{\mathcal{C}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\sigma)\,\overline{F_{2}(\sigma)}F_{2}(\sigma)-\int\mu_{v^{\varepsilon}}(\mathrm{d}\xi)\,\overline{f_{2}(\xi)}f_{2}(\xi)\bigg|\lesssim_{\kappa,v}\frac{\nu^{1/2}\vee\nu^{2-s}}{\varepsilon^{5d+1}}\,.

Lemma 6.10
.

Uniformly in
\eta>0
, we have

\bigg|\int\mu_{\mathcal{C}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\sigma)\,\overline{f_{2}(\langle\sigma\rangle)}F_{2}(\sigma)-\int\mu_{v^{\varepsilon}}(\mathrm{d}\xi)\,\overline{f_{2}(\xi)}f_{2}(\xi)\bigg|\lesssim_{\kappa,v}\frac{\nu^{1/2}\vee\nu^{2-s}}{\varepsilon^{5d+1}}\,.

Since the proofs of Lemmas
6.9
and
6.10
are similar, we focus our discussion only on showing Lemma
6.9
. We start by recalling some useful notation from
(
7
, Section 5.1)
.

Definition 6.11

(Classical interactions)
.

Let
x,\tilde{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\omega\in\Omega^{\tau_{1},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}
,
\tilde{\omega}\in\Omega^{\tau_{2},\tilde{\tau}_{2}}
be continuous paths. We define the
point-point interaction

\bigl(\mathbb{V}^{\varepsilon}\bigr)_{x,\tilde{x}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\int\mu_{v^{\varepsilon}}(\mathrm{d}\xi)\,\xi(x)\xi(\tilde{x})=v^{\varepsilon}(x-\tilde{x})

the
point-path interaction

\bigl(\mathbb{V}^{\varepsilon}\bigr)_{x}(\omega)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\int\mu_{v^{\varepsilon}}(\mathrm{d}\xi)\int\mathrm{d}s\,\xi(\omega(s))=\int\mathrm{d}s\,v^{\varepsilon}(x-\omega(s))

and the
path-path interaction

\mathbb{V}^{\varepsilon}(\omega,\tilde{\omega})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\int\mu_{v^{\varepsilon}}(\mathrm{d}\xi)\int\mathrm{d}s\,\xi(\omega(s))\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{s}\,\xi(\tilde{\omega}(\tilde{s}))=\int\mathrm{d}s\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{s}\,v^{\varepsilon}(\omega(s)-\tilde{\omega}(\tilde{s}))\,.

We use the notation:
x_{i,0}\equiv x_{i},\tilde{x}_{i,0}\equiv\tilde{x}_{i}
for
i=1,2
, and

A\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\{2,3\}\times\{0,1\}.

Following
(
6
, (5.8)-(5.9))
, we have

\int\mu_{v^{\varepsilon}}(\mathrm{d}\xi)\,\overline{f_{2}(\xi)}f_{2}(\xi)=\int_{[0,\infty)^{3}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{r}\,\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa|\mathbf{r}|}}{|\mathbf{r}|}\int_{[0,\infty)^{3}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{r}}\,\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa|\tilde{\mathbf{r}}|}}{|\tilde{\mathbf{r}}|}I^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{r},\tilde{\mathbf{r}})

(6.31)

where

\displaystyle I^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{r},\tilde{\mathbf{r}})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{x}}\int{\mathbf{W}}_{x_{1},x_{3}}^{r_{3},0}(\mathrm{d}\omega_{3}){\mathbf{W}}_{x_{3},x_{2}}^{r_{2},0}(\mathrm{d}\omega_{2}){\mathbf{W}}_{x_{2},x_{1}}^{r_{1},0}(\mathrm{d}\omega_{1}){\mathbf{W}}_{\tilde{x}_{1},\tilde{x}_{3}}^{\tilde{r}_{3},0}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\omega}_{3})

(6.32)

\displaystyle\times{\mathbf{W}}_{\tilde{x}_{3},\tilde{x}_{2}}^{\tilde{r}_{2},0}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\omega}_{2}){\mathbf{W}}_{\tilde{x}_{2},\tilde{x}_{1}}^{\tilde{r}_{1},0}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\omega}_{1})\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{1}{2}\bigl(\mathbb{V}^{\varepsilon}(\omega_{1},\omega_{1})+\mathbb{V}^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{\omega}_{1},\tilde{\omega}_{1})-2\mathbb{V}^{\varepsilon}(\omega_{1},\tilde{\omega}_{1})\bigr)}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\Pi\in\mathfrak{M}(A)}\prod_{\{a,b\}\in\Pi}(\mathbb{V}^{\varepsilon})_{x_{a},x_{b}}\prod_{a\in A\backslash[\Pi]}\mathrm{i}\bigl((\mathbb{V}^{\varepsilon})_{x_{a}}(\omega_{1})-(\mathbb{V}^{\varepsilon})_{x_{a}}(\tilde{\omega}_{1})\bigr)\,,

Here we defined
[\Pi]\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\{1,\dots,\Pi\}
, and
\mathfrak{M}(A)
