the set of partial pairings of the set
A
. Moreover, we introduced the notation

{\mathbf{W}}^{t,s}_{y,x}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}={\mathbb{W}}^{t,s}_{y,x}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{s}^{t}\mathrm{d}r\,U^{\varepsilon}_{\eta}(\omega(r))}=\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}\psi^{t-s}(x-y){{\mathfrak{P}}}^{t,s}_{y,x}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\,.

(6.33)

Definition 6.12

(Quantum interactions)
.

Let
(\tau,x),(\tilde{\tau},\tilde{x})\in[0,\nu]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
and let
\omega\in\Omega^{\tau_{1},\tilde{\tau}_{1}},\tilde{\omega}\in

\Omega^{\tau_{2},\tilde{\tau}_{2}}
be continuous paths. With
\delta_{\eta}
given by (
5.1
), we define the
point-point interaction

\bigl(\mathbb{V}_{\eta}\bigr)_{x,\tilde{x}}^{\tau,\tilde{\tau}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\int\mu_{\mathcal{C}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\sigma)\,\sigma(\tau,x)\sigma(\tilde{\tau},\tilde{x})=\nu\delta_{\eta}(\tau-\tilde{\tau})v^{\varepsilon}(x-\tilde{x})\,,

the
point-point interaction

\displaystyle\bigl(\mathbb{V}_{\eta}\bigr)_{x}^{\tau}(\omega)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\int\mu_{\mathcal{C}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\sigma)\,\sigma(\tau,x)\int_{0}^{\nu}\mathrm{d}t\int\mathrm{d}s\,\delta(t-[s]_{\nu})\sigma(t,\omega(s))

\displaystyle=\nu\int\mathrm{d}s\,\delta_{\eta}(\tau-[s]_{\nu})v^{\varepsilon}(x-\omega(s))\,,

and the
path-path interaction

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}_{\eta}(\omega,\tilde{\omega})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\int\mu_{\mathcal{C}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\sigma)\int_{0}^{\nu}\mathrm{d}t\int\mathrm{d}s\,\delta(t-[s]_{\nu})\sigma(t,\omega(s))\int_{0}^{\nu}\mathrm{d}\tilde{t}\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{s}\,\delta(\tilde{t}-[\tilde{s}]_{\nu})\sigma(\tilde{t},\tilde{\omega}(\tilde{s}))

\displaystyle=\nu\int\mathrm{d}s\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{s}\,\delta_{\eta}([s]_{\nu}-[\tilde{s}]_{\nu})v^{\varepsilon}(\omega(s)-\tilde{\omega}(\tilde{s}))\,.

Recalling (
5.12
), and proceeding as in
(
6
, (5.17)–(5.18), (5.21))
, we have

\int\mu_{\mathcal{C}_{\eta}}(\mathrm{d}\sigma)\,\overline{F_{2}(\sigma)}F_{2}(\sigma)=\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in(\nu\mathbb{N})^{3}}\frac{\mathbbold{1}_{|\mathbf{r}|>0}\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa|\mathbf{r}|}}{|\mathbf{r}|}\sum_{\tilde{\mathbf{r}}\in(\nu\mathbb{N})^{3}}\frac{\mathbbold{1}_{|\tilde{\mathbf{r}}|>0}\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa|\tilde{\mathbf{r}}|}}{|\tilde{\mathbf{r}}|}J(\mathbf{r},\tilde{\mathbf{r}})

(6.34)

where

\displaystyle J(\mathbf{r},\tilde{\mathbf{r}})

(6.35)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\int_{[0,\nu]^{3}}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\tau}\int_{[0,\nu]^{3}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\tau}}\int\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbf{x}}\int{\mathbf{W}}_{x_{1},x_{3}}^{\tau_{1}+r_{3},\tau_{3}}(\mathrm{d}\omega_{3}){\mathbf{W}}_{x_{3},x_{2}}^{\tau_{3}+r_{2},\tau_{2}}(\mathrm{d}\omega_{2}){\mathbf{W}}_{x_{2},x_{1}}^{\tau_{2}+r_{1},\tau_{1}}(\mathrm{d}\omega_{1})

\displaystyle\times{\mathbf{W}}_{\tilde{x}_{1},\tilde{x}_{3}}^{\tilde{\tau}_{1}+\tilde{r}_{3},\tilde{\tau}_{3}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\omega}_{3}){\mathbf{W}}_{\tilde{x}_{3},\tilde{x}_{2}}^{\tilde{\tau}_{3}+\tilde{r}_{2},\tilde{\tau}_{2}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\omega}_{2}){\mathbf{W}}_{\tilde{x}_{2},\tilde{x}_{1}}^{\tilde{\tau}_{2}+\tilde{r}_{1},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\omega}_{1})\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{1}{2}(\mathbb{V}_{\eta}(\omega_{1},\omega_{1})+\mathbb{V}_{\eta}(\tilde{\omega}_{1},\tilde{\omega}_{1})-2\mathbb{V}_{\eta}(\omega_{1},\tilde{\omega}_{1}))}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\Pi\in\mathfrak{M}(A)}\prod_{\{a,b\}\in\Pi}(\mathbb{V}_{\eta})_{x_{a},x_{b}}^{\tau_{a},\tau_{b}}\prod_{a\in A\backslash[\Pi]}\mathrm{i}\bigl((\mathbb{V}_{\eta})_{x_{a}}^{\tau_{a}}(\omega_{1})-(\mathbb{V}_{\eta})_{x_{a}}^{\tau_{a}}(\tilde{\omega}_{1})\bigr)\,.

In order to prove Lemma
6.9
, we first need to compare (
6.32
) with (
6.35
). As a result we have the following statement.

Lemma 6.13
.

For all
\mathbf{r},\mathbf{\tilde{r}}\in(\nu\mathbb{N})^{3}
with
\lvert\mathbf{r}\rvert,\lvert\mathbf{\tilde{r}}\rvert>0
, uniformly in

we have

\big|J(\mathbf{r},\tilde{\mathbf{r}})-\nu^{6}I^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{r},\tilde{\mathbf{r}})\big|\\
\lesssim\frac{\nu^{13/2}}{\varepsilon^{5d+1}}\Bigl(\tr\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-\lvert{\mathbf{r}}\rvert(-\Delta/2+U/2)}\bigr)+\frac{1}{|\mathbf{r}|^{d/2}}\Bigr)\Bigl(\tr\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-|\tilde{\mathbf{r}|}(-\Delta/2+U/2)}\bigr)+\frac{1}{|{\tilde{\mathbf{r}}|^{d/2}}}\Bigr)(1+|{\mathbf{r}}|+|\tilde{\mathbf{r}}|)^{6}\,.

Finally, Lemma
6.9
follows from controlling the difference between Riemann sums and the corresponding integral.

Lemma 6.14
.

Recall (
6.32
). Under the assumptions of Remark
2.3
, we have that uniformly in
\eta>0

\bigg|\int_{[0,\infty)^{3}}\mathrm{\penalty\ d}\mathbf{r}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa|\mathbf{r}|}}{|\mathbf{r}|}\int_{[0,\infty)^{3}}\mathrm{\penalty\ d}\tilde{\mathbf{r}}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa|\tilde{\mathbf{r}}|}}{|\tilde{\mathbf{r}}|}I^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{r},\tilde{\mathbf{r}})\\
-\nu^{6}\sum_{\mathbf{r}\in(\nu\mathbb{N})^{3}}\frac{\mathbbold{1}_{|\mathbf{r}|>0}\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa|\mathbf{r}|}}{|\mathbf{r}|}\sum_{\tilde{\mathbf{r}}\in(\nu\mathbb{N})^{3}}\frac{\mathbbold{1}_{|\tilde{\mathbf{r}}|>0}\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa|\tilde{\mathbf{r}}|}}{|\tilde{\mathbf{r}}|}I^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{r},\tilde{\mathbf{r}})\bigg|\lesssim_{\kappa,v}\frac{\nu^{2-s}}{\varepsilon^{5d+1}}\,.

(6.36)

We dedicate the rest of the subsection to prove (
6.13
) and Lemma
6.14
.
To start we recall the following result from
7
regarding path-path interactions.

Proposition 6.15

(
(
7
, lemma C.1)
)
.

Consider two continuous paths
\omega\in\Omega^{\tau_{1},\tilde{\tau}_{1}}
,
\tilde{\omega}\in\Omega^{\tau_{2},\tilde{\tau}_{2}}
. Then, uniformly in
\eta>0
,

\big|\mathbb{V}_{\eta}(\omega,\tilde{\omega})-\bigl(\mathbb{V}^{\varepsilon}(\omega,\tilde{\omega})+\hat{\mathbb{V}}_{\eta}(\omega,\tilde{\omega})\bigr)\big|\lesssim\frac{\nu}{\varepsilon^{d}}(\tau_{1}-\tilde{\tau}_{1})

where

\big|\hat{\mathbb{V}}_{\eta}(\omega,\tilde{\omega})\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d+1}}\int\mathrm{d}s\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{I_{i}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{s}\int_{I_{i}}\mathrm{d}\hat{s}\,|\tilde{\omega}(\tilde{s})-\tilde{\omega}(\hat{s})|\delta_{\eta}(\{s\}_{\nu}-\{\hat{s}\}_{\nu})\,,

(6.37)

for
N\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\big\lceil\frac{\tau_{2}-\tilde{\tau}_{2}}{\nu}\big\rceil
and

I_{i}=\begin{cases}[\tilde{\tau}_{2}+(i-1)\nu,\tilde{\tau}_{2}+i\nu),i=1,\dots,N-1\\
[\tilde{\tau}_{2}+(N-1)\nu,\tau_{2}],i=N\,.\end{cases}

The rest of this subsection closely follows the arguments of
(
7
, Section 5)
, with any modifications arising from the need to bound quantities of the form

\int{\mathbf{W}}_{x,x}^{|\mathbf{r}|,0}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\,|{\omega}(\tilde{s})-{\omega}(\hat{s})|

(6.38)

for times
\hat{s},\tilde{s}
satisfying
\lvert\hat{s}-\tilde{s}\rvert\leqslant\nu
, by an integrable function of
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. For this purpose we show the following result.

Lemma 6.16
.

Let
\omega\in\Omega_{x_{1},x_{1}}^{\tau_{1}+|\mathbf{r}|,\tau_{1}}
be a Brownian loop of length
\lvert r\rvert\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3}
, with
r_{i}\in\nu\mathbb{N}
for
i=1,2,3
, and
{\omega}_{1}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}={\left.\kern-1.2pt{\omega}\vphantom{\big|}\right|_{[{\tau}_{1},{r}_{1}+{\tau}_{2}]}}
its restriction between the times
\tau_{1}
and
r_{1}+\tau_{2}
. Then

\int_{[0,\nu]^{3}}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\tau}\int_{[0,\nu]^{3}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\tau}}\int\mathrm{d}x_{1}\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{1}\int{\mathbf{W}}_{x_{1},x_{1}}^{\tau_{1}+|\mathbf{r}|,\tau_{1}}(\mathrm{d}\omega){\mathbf{W}}_{\tilde{x}_{1},\tilde{x}_{1}}^{\tilde{\tau}_{1}+|\tilde{\mathbf{r}}|,\tilde{\tau}_{1}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\omega})\big|\hat{\mathbb{V}}_{\eta}(\omega_{1},\omega_{1})\big|\\
\lesssim\frac{\nu^{13/2}}{\varepsilon^{d+1}}\tr{\Bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-\lvert\tilde{\mathbf{r}}\rvert(-\Delta/2+U/2)}\Bigr)}\frac{(1+{|\mathbf{r}|)^{2}}}{|\mathbf{r}|^{d/2}}\,.

(6.39)

Proof.

Using Lemma
5.11
, (
6.37
), the definition of
\delta_{\eta}
(
5.1
), in particular
\int\delta_{\eta}=1
, and Fubini’s theorem, we find that the left-hand side of (
6.39
) is bounded by

\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{d+1}}\tr\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-|\tilde{\mathbf{r}}|(-\Delta/2+U/2)}\bigr)\\
\times\int_{[0,\nu]^{3}}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\tau}\int_{[0,\nu]^{3}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\tau}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\int_{I_{i}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{s}\int_{I_{i}}\mathrm{d}\hat{s}\int\mathrm{d}x_{1}\int{\mathbf{W}}_{x_{1},x_{1}}^{\tau_{1}+|\mathbf{r}|,\tau_{1}}(\mathrm{d}\omega)|{\omega_{1}}(\tilde{s})-{\omega_{1}}(\hat{s})|\,.

(6.40)

We now bound the distance between two positions of the Brownian bridge
\omega_{1}
in a time interval of size
\nu
by Lemma
5.10

(ii)
, by integrating over the final position
x_{2}
of
\omega_{1}\vcentcolon[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}+r_{1}]\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and obtain

\begin{split}&\int{\mathbf{W}}_{x_{1},x_{1}}^{\tau_{1}+|\mathbf{r}|,\tau_{1}}(\mathrm{d}\omega)|{\omega_{1}}(\tilde{s})-{\omega_{1}}(\hat{s})|\\
&\quad=\int\mathrm{d}x_{2}\int{\mathbf{W}}_{x_{1},x_{2}}^{\tau_{1}+|\mathbf{r}|,\tau_{2}+r_{1}}(\mathrm{d}\omega_{2})\int{\mathbf{W}}_{x_{2},x_{1}}^{\tau_{2}+r_{1},\tau_{1}}(\mathrm{d}\omega_{1})|{\omega_{1}}(\tilde{s})-{\omega_{1}}(\hat{s})|\\
&\quad\lesssim\int\mathrm{d}x_{2}\,\int{\mathfrak{P}}_{x_{1},x_{2}}^{t_{2},0}(\mathrm{d}\omega_{2})\psi^{t_{2}}(x_{1}-x_{2})\psi^{t_{1}}(x_{1}-x_{2})\Bigl(|\hat{s}-\tilde{s}|^{1/2}+\lvert x_{1}-x_{2}\rvert\frac{|\hat{s}-\tilde{s}|}{{t_{1}}}\Bigr)\end{split}

(6.41)

where
t_{1}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=r_{1}+\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}
and
t_{2}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\lvert\mathbf{r}\rvert-t_{1}
are the lengths of the time domains of
\omega_{1}
and
\omega_{2}
respectively and we recall the notation (
6.33
). Using heat kernel estimates Lemma
7.5
with
g(x)=1+\lvert x\rvert^{\theta}
,

\int{\mathfrak{P}}_{x_{1},x_{2}}^{t_{2},0}(\mathrm{d}\omega_{2})\leqslant\mathbbold{1}_{t_{2}\leqslant|x_{1}|^{-2(\theta+1)}}+\mathrm{e}^{-c|x_{1}|^{\theta}t_{2}}+\mathrm{e}^{-c|x_{1}|^{1+\theta/2}}+\mathrm{e}^{-c(\sqrt{t_{2}}|x_{1}|^{\theta+1})^{2/3}}\,,

and integrating in
x_{1}
, we obtain the bound

\int\mathrm{d}x_{1}\int{\mathfrak{P}}_{x_{1},x_{2}}^{t_{2},0}(\mathrm{d}\omega_{2})\lesssim_{d}1+t_{2}^{d/(2(\theta+1))}+t_{2}^{-d/\theta}+t_{2}^{-d/(2(\theta+1))}\,.

(6.42)

We can now compute the two integrals involving the heat kernel appearing on the right-hand side of (
6.41
)

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}x_{2}\,\psi^{t_{2}}(x_{1}-x_{2})\psi^{t_{1}}(x_{1}-x_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{(t_{1}+t_{2})^{d/2}}=\frac{1}{\lvert\mathbf{r}\rvert^{d/2}}\,,

(6.43)

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}x_{2}\,\psi^{t_{2}}(x_{1}-x_{2})\psi^{t_{1}}(x_{1}-x_{2})\lvert x_{1}-x_{2}\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{t_{1}t_{2}}}{{(t_{1}+t_{2})^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}}=\frac{\sqrt{t_{1}t_{2}}}{{\lvert\mathbf{r}\rvert^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}}\,.

(6.44)

Notice that by definition, for any
\hat{s},\tilde{s}\in I_{i},i=1\dots N
we have
\lvert\hat{s}-\tilde{s}\rvert\leqslant\nu
and
\nu N\lesssim t_{1}
. Combining this bound together with (
6.40
)-(
6.44
), we have

\int\mathrm{d}x_{1}\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{1}\int{\mathbf{W}}_{x_{1},x_{1}}^{\tau_{1}+|\mathbf{r}|,\tau_{1}}(\mathrm{d}\omega){\mathbf{W}}_{\tilde{x}_{1},\tilde{x}_{1}}^{\tilde{\tau}_{1}+|\tilde{\mathbf{r}}|,\tilde{\tau}_{1}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\omega})\big|\hat{\mathbb{V}}_{\eta}(\omega_{1},\omega_{1})\big|\\
\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d+1}}\tr\Bigl({\mathrm{e}^{-\lvert\tilde{\mathbf{r}}\rvert(-\Delta/2+U/2)}}\Bigr)\bigl(1+t_{2}^{d/(2(\theta+1))}+t_{2}^{-d/\theta}+t_{2}^{-d/(2(\theta+1))}\bigr)\\
\times\nu t_{1}\biggl(\frac{\sqrt{\nu}}{\lvert\mathbf{r}\rvert^{d/2}}+\frac{\nu\sqrt{t_{1}t_{2}}}{t_{1}\lvert\mathbf{r}\rvert^{(d+1)/2}}\biggr)\,.

(6.45)

Moreover, using that
\nu\leqslant\lvert\mathbf{r}\rvert
,
t_{1},t_{2}\leqslant\lvert\mathbf{r}\rvert
, we can further obtain

\eqref{eq:boundwitht2t1}\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d+1}}\tr\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-|\tilde{\mathbf{r}}|(-\Delta/2+U/2)}\bigr)\Bigl(1+t_{2}^{d/(2(\theta+1))}+t_{2}^{-d/\theta}+t_{2}^{-d/(2(\theta+1))}\Bigr)\nu^{3/2}\biggl(\frac{1}{|\mathbf{r}|^{d/2-1}}\biggr)\,.

(6.46)

We now integrate over the variables
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}
, and, since from Remark
2.3
we have
1-d/\theta>0
, we find

\displaystyle\int_{0\leqslant\tau_{2},\tau_{1}\leqslant\nu}\mathrm{d}\tau_{1}\,\mathrm{d}\tau_{2}\Bigl(1+t_{2}^{d/(2(\theta+1))}+t_{2}^{-d/\theta}+t_{2}^{-d/(2(\theta+1))}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\theta}\nu^{2}+\nu^{2}|\mathbf{r}|+\int_{0}^{\nu}\mathrm{d}\tau_{2}\,(|\mathbf{r}|+\nu-\tau_{2})^{1-d/\theta}

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu(1+|\mathbf{r}|)\,.

We thus obtain (
6.39
) by integrating over the variables
\tau_{3}
and
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\tau}}
.
∎

We conclude this subsection by we showing (
6.13
) and Lemma
6.14
.

Proof of (
6.13
).

The proof proceeds along the same lines as in
(
7
, lemma 5.15)
, where we replace the estimates involving
\hat{\mathbb{V}}_{\eta}
with (
6.39
). For times
s,t\geqslant 0
satisfying
\lvert s-t\rvert\leqslant\nu
, we can prove as in Lemma
6.16
that

\nu\int_{[0,\nu]^{3}}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\tau}\int_{[0,\nu]^{3}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\tau}}\int\mathrm{d}x_{1}\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}_{1}\int{\mathbf{W}}_{x_{1},x_{1}}^{\tau_{1}+|\mathbf{r}|,\tau_{1}}(\mathrm{d}\omega){\mathbf{W}}_{\tilde{x}_{1},\tilde{x}_{1}}^{\tilde{\tau}_{1}+|\tilde{\mathbf{r}}|,\tilde{\tau}_{1}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{\omega})|\omega(s)-\omega(t)|\\
\lesssim\nu^{13/2}\tr\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-|\tilde{\mathbf{r}}|(-\Delta/2+U/2)}\bigr)\frac{(1+|\mathbf{r}|)^{2}}{|\mathbf{r}|^{d/2}}\,,

(6.47)

which allows us to conclude the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
6.14
.

We can show this result following the steps of the proof Lemma
6.8
by noting that

\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{e}^{-\lvert\mathbf{r}\rvert t}=\frac{1}{\lvert\mathbf{r}\rvert}
, and

|I^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{r},\mathbf{\tilde{r}})|\lesssim\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{4d}}\tr\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-\lvert\mathbf{r}\rvert(-\Delta/2+U/2)}\bigr)\tr\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{-\lvert\mathbf{\tilde{r}}\rvert(-\Delta/2+U/2)}\bigr)(1+|\mathbf{r}|+|\mathbf{\tilde{r}}|)^{4}\,,

(6.48)

combined with the estimates
(
7
, proof of lemma 5.15, claims (i)-(iii))
and (
6.47
).
∎

7. Regularity of the Green function

In this section we state and prove estimates on the Green function
G
from (
2.5
) of the Hamiltonian (
2.1
) and its regularised version
G_{N}
from (
2.6
) with exponential cutoff function
\vartheta(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\mathrm{e}^{-x}
, as well as estimates on the gradient of
G
. As an application, we prove bounds on the function
\tau^{\varepsilon}
from (
2.17
). Since the cutoff function is exponential, we have

G_{N}=\frac{1}{h}\mathrm{e}^{-h/N}=\int_{\frac{1}{N}}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{e}^{-th}\,,\qquad G_{\infty}=G\,.

(7.1)

Throughout this section,
g\vcentcolon\mathbb{R}^{d}\to[1,\infty)
is a positive, radial, non-decreasing function satisfying
g\leqslant U
, and for fixed
0<\gamma<1
we define
\tilde{g}
as in (
4.1
) .

The first main result of this section gives bounds on
G
and the difference
G_{N}-G
.

Proposition 7.1

(Estimates on
G_{N}
)
.

Let
d=2
. For
0<N\leqslant\infty
, let
G_{N}
the truncated Green function (
7.1
). Then for any fixed
0<\gamma<1
there exists a constant
c\equiv c_{\gamma}>0
such that

G_{N}(x,y)\lesssim\Bigl[\Bigl(\bigl[\log\Bigl(|x-y|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)\vee\tilde{g}(y)}\Bigr)^{-1}\bigr]_{+}\wedge\log N\Bigr)\vee 1\Bigr]\mathrm{e}^{-c|x-y|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)\vee\tilde{g}(y)}}

(7.2)

and

|G_{N}(x,y)-G(x,y)|\lesssim\Bigl[\log(N|x-y|^{2})^{-1}\Bigr]_{+}\wedge\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-N|x-y|^{2}}}{N|x-y|^{2}}\,.

(7.3)

An upper bound similar to (
7.2
) was previously obtained in
2
. The estimate (
7.2
) is a generalisation of this result to potentials with faster radial growth and to finite
N
.

Remark 7.2
.

By the same proof, the result of Proposition
7.1
holds in any dimension
d\geqslant 3
with bound

{G(x,y)\lesssim|x-y|^{2-d}\mathrm{e}^{-c|x-y|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)\vee\tilde{g}(y)}}\,.}

(7.4)

The second main result of this section shows that under Assumption
2.1
,
G
is continuously differentiable with a bound on its gradient.

Proposition 7.3

(Estimate on
\nabla G
)
.

Fix
d\geqslant 2
and
0<\gamma<1
. Suppose that
U
satisfies Assumption
2.1
. Then there exists a constant
c\equiv c_{\gamma}>0
such that
G
is differentiable for all
x\neq y
and

|\nabla_{x}G(x,y)|\lesssim\frac{1}{\lvert x-y\rvert^{d-1}}\mathrm{e}^{-c\lvert x-y\rvert\sqrt{\tilde{g}(y)}}\,.

(7.5)

Remark 7.4
.

For any positive locally bounded potential
U
, we have from Proposition
7.1
that
U\cdot G
is
L^{p}_{\text{loc}}(\Omega)
, for any
p\in[1,\infty]
and
\Omega\subset\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{(x,\cdots,x)\vcentcolon x\in\mathbb{R}\}
. Using Proposition
7.3
, Calderón-Zygmund estimates and Sobolev embedding with
p>d
, we find that

x\mapsto\nabla_{x}G(x,y)

is continuous for all
x\neq y
.

The rest of this section is devoted to the proofs of Proposition
7.1
and Proposition
7.3
.

7.1.  Proof of Proposition
7.1

To prove Proposition
7.1
we first need the following results from
(
6
, Section 7)
. Notice that these are modified versions of Lemma 7.3 and Lemma 7.4 in that paper. In particular, Lemma
7.5
is a more general version of
(
6
, Lemma 7.3)
, and the proof follows using a similar stopping time argument which we omit here.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
d\geqslant 1
. For any fixed
\gamma\in(0,1)
there exist constants
C,c>0
such that

\begin{split}&\int\mathbb{W}_{x,\tilde{x}}^{t,0}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(\omega(s))}\,\\
&\quad\leqslant C\psi^{t}(x-\tilde{x})\mathrm{e}^{-c\frac{t}{2}}\Bigl(\mathbbm{1}_{t\leqslant(g(\gamma x)\vee g(\gamma\tilde{x}))^{-2}(|x|+|\tilde{x}|)^{-2}}+\mathrm{e}^{-c(g(\gamma x)\vee g(\gamma\tilde{x}))t}\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt+\mathrm{e}^{-c(|x|+|\tilde{x}|)\sqrt{g(\gamma x)\vee g(\gamma\tilde{x})}}+\mathrm{e}^{-ct^{1/3}(|x|+|\tilde{x}|)^{2/3}(g(\gamma x)\vee g(\gamma\tilde{x}))^{2/3}}\Bigr)\end{split}

(7.6)

for all
x,\tilde{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
t>0
.

Proof of Proposition
7.1
.

We start with the proof of (
7.2
). Without loss of generality we can assume
|x|\geqslant|y|
. We also assume that
|x|\geqslant 1
, for otherwise the statement is trivial. Hence, it is sufficient to prove that

G_{N}(x,y)\lesssim\Bigl[\Bigl(\Bigl[\log\Bigl(|x-y|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)}\Bigr)^{-1}\Bigr]_{+}\wedge\log N\Bigr)\vee 1\Bigr]\mathrm{e}^{-c|x-y|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)}}\,.

The Feynman-Kac formula from Lemma
5.11
yields

\begin{split}G_{N}(x,y)&=\int_{\frac{1}{N}}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{e}^{-th}(x,y)=\int_{\frac{1}{N}}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa t}\int\mathbb{W}_{x,y}^{t,0}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\,\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(\omega(s))},\end{split}

from which we deduce using (
7.6
) that

\displaystyle G_{N}(x,y)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\frac{1}{N}}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\frac{1}{t}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{2t}}\mathrm{e}^{-c\frac{t}{2}}\Bigl(\mathbbm{1}_{t\leqslant\tilde{g}(x)^{-2}|x|^{-2}}+\mathrm{e}^{-c\tilde{g}(x)t}+\mathrm{e}^{-c|x|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)}}+\mathrm{e}^{-ct^{1/3}|x|^{2/3}\tilde{g}(x)^{2/3}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}\text{(a)}+\text{(b)}+\text{(c)}+\text{(d)}\,.

(7.7)

We analyse the four terms separately. First we consider the term (a):

\begin{split}\text{(a)}\lesssim\int_{\frac{1}{N}}^{\tilde{g}(x)^{-2}|x|^{-2}}\mathrm{d}t\,\frac{1}{t}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{2t}}&=\,\int_{\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{2}\tilde{g}(x)^{2}|x|^{2}}^{\frac{N|x-y|^{2}}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-s}\,.\end{split}

We consider different cases: if
|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)\leqslant 2
and
|x-y|^{2}\leqslant\frac{2}{N}
then

\text{(a)}\lesssim\log(\frac{\tilde{g}(x)^{2}|x|^{2}}{N})^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-c|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)}\lesssim\log N\mathrm{e}^{-c|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)}\,.

(7.8)

On the other hand, if
|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)\leqslant 2
and
|x-y|^{2}>\frac{2}{N}
then

\begin{split}\text{(a)}&\lesssim\int_{\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{2}\tilde{g}(x)^{2}|x|^{2}}^{1}\,\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-s}+\int_{1}^{\frac{N|x-y|^{2}}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-s}\\
&\lesssim\Bigl(\Bigl[-\log(|x-y|\tilde{g}(x)|x|)\Bigr]_{+}+1\Bigr)\mathrm{e}^{-c|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)^{2}|x|^{2}}\\
&\lesssim\Bigl(\Bigl[-\log(|x-y|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)})\Bigr]_{+}+1\Bigr)\mathrm{e}^{-c|x-y|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)}}\,.\end{split}

(7.9)

On the other hand, if
|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)>2
, we have
\tilde{g}(x)|x|^{2}\geqslant\tilde{g}(x)|x-y|^{2}>2
, which also implies
|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)^{2}|x|^{2}>2
. Thus,

\text{(a)}\lesssim\int_{\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{2}\tilde{g}(x)^{2}|x|^{2}}^{\frac{N|x-y|^{2}}{2}}\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{e}^{-s}\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{-c|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)^{2}|x|^{2}}\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{-c|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)}\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{-c|x-y|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)}}\,.

(7.10)

Together (
7.8
), (
7.9
), and (
7.10
) are all bounded by the right-hand side of (
7.2
).

Next, we consider the term (b) in (
8
). We perform a change of variable
s=\tilde{g}(x)t
and use that
\tilde{g}(x)+\frac{1}{2}\geqslant\tilde{g}(x)
, which yields

\begin{split}\text{(b)}&=\int_{\frac{1}{N}}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\frac{1}{t}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{2t}}\mathrm{e}^{-c\tilde{g}(x)t-c\frac{t}{2}}\\
&\lesssim\int_{\frac{\tilde{g}(x)}{N}}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)}{2s}}\mathrm{e}^{-s}\\
&=\int_{\frac{\tilde{g}(x)}{N}}^{1}\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{c|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)}{2s}}\mathrm{e}^{-s}+\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{c|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)}{2s}}\mathrm{e}^{-s}\,,\end{split}

(7.11)

where, without loss of generality, we assumed
\frac{\tilde{g}(x)}{N}\leqslant 1
. Notice that, whenever
\frac{\tilde{g}(x)}{N}>1
, we can bound
(b)
with the second integral on the right-hand side on the last line of (
7.11
).
We perform another change of variables in the first integral on the right-hand side of (
7.11
) to get

\begin{split}\int_{\frac{\tilde{g}(x)}{N}}^{1}\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{c|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)}{2s}}\mathrm{e}^{-s}&\lesssim\int_{\frac{\tilde{g}(x)}{N}}^{1}\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{c|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)}{2s}}\\
&=\int_{c|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)}^{cN|x-y|^{2}}\mathrm{d}t\,\frac{1}{t}\mathrm{e}^{-t}=\int_{c|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,\frac{1}{t}\mathrm{e}^{-t}+\int_{1}^{cN|x-y|^{2}}\mathrm{d}t\,\frac{1}{t}\mathrm{e}^{-t}\end{split}

(7.12)

for
cN|x-y|^{2}>1
. Hence,

\begin{split}\int_{c|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,\frac{1}{t}\mathrm{e}^{-t}+\int_{1}^{cN|x-y|^{2}}\mathrm{d}t\,\frac{1}{t}\mathrm{e}^{-t}&\lesssim\bigl([-\log(|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x))]_{+}+1\bigr)\mathrm{e}^{-c|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)}\,.\end{split}

If
cN|x-y|^{2}\leqslant 1
then the right-hand side of (
7.12
) can be bounded as

\int_{c|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)}^{cN|x-y|^{2}}\mathrm{d}t\,\frac{1}{t}\mathrm{e}^{-t}\lesssim\log\biggl(\frac{\tilde{g}(x)}{N}\biggr)^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{-c|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)}\lesssim\log N\mathrm{e}^{-c|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)}\,.

The second integral in (
7.11
) can be estimated using an elementary argument by

\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{c|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)}{2s}}\mathrm{e}^{-s}\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{-c|x-y|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)}}\,.

Next, we consider the term (c) in (
8
). We argue analogously to (
7.11
), (
7.12
). Using
|x-y|\leqslant 2|x|
we get

\begin{split}&\int_{\frac{1}{N}}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\frac{1}{t}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{2t}}\mathrm{e}^{-c|x|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)}}\mathrm{e}^{-c\frac{t}{2}}\\
&\quad\lesssim\Bigl(\Bigl(\bigl[\log|x-y|^{-1}\bigr]_{+}\wedge\log N\Bigr)\vee 1\Bigr)\mathrm{e}^{-c|x|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)}}\\
&\quad=\Bigl(\Bigl(\Bigl[\log(|x-y|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)})^{-1}+\log(\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)})\Bigr]_{+}\wedge\log N\Bigr)\vee 1\Bigr)\mathrm{e}^{-c|x|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)}}\\
&\quad\lesssim\Bigl(\Bigl(\Bigl[\log(|x-y|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)})^{-1}\Bigr]_{+}\wedge\log N\Bigr)\vee 1\Bigr)\mathrm{e}^{-c|x|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)}}\,,\end{split}

where in the last step we used that
\log(\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)})\mathrm{e}^{-c/2\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)}}
is of order one.

Finally, we consider the term (d) in (
8
). This time we perform the change of variables
s=t(\tilde{g}(x)|x|)^{2}
to get

\begin{split}\int_{\frac{1}{N}}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\frac{1}{t}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{2t}}\mathrm{e}^{-ct^{1/3}(|x|\tilde{g}(x))^{2/3}}&=\int_{\frac{(\tilde{g}(x)|x|)^{2}}{N}}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{c|x-y|^{2}(\tilde{g}(x)|x|)^{2}}{s}}\mathrm{e}^{-s^{1/3}}\\
&\lesssim\int_{\frac{(\tilde{g}(x)|x|)^{2}}{N}}^{1}\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{c\lambda}{s}}+\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{c\lambda}{s}}\mathrm{e}^{-s^{1/3}}\,,\\
&=(\mathrm{d}_{1})+(\mathrm{d}_{2})\,,\end{split}

where we set
\lambda=|x-y|^{2}(\tilde{g}(x)|x|)^{2}\geqslant|x-y|^{4}\tilde{g}(x)^{2}
and assuming (without loss of generality, similarly as in (
7.11
)) that
\frac{\tilde{g}(x)}{N}\leqslant 1
. For the term
(\mathrm{d}_{1})
, proceeding along the same lines as before, if
|x-y|^{2}>\frac{2}{N}
then

\begin{split}(\mathrm{d}_{1})&=\int_{\lambda}^{\frac{N|x-y|^{2}}{2}}\mathrm{d}u\,\frac{1}{u}\mathrm{e}^{-u}=\int_{\lambda}^{1}\mathrm{d}u\,\frac{1}{u}\mathrm{e}^{-u}+\int_{1}^{\frac{N|x-y|^{2}}{2}}\mathrm{d}u\,\frac{1}{u}\mathrm{e}^{-u}\lesssim\bigl([-\log(\lambda)]_{+}+1\bigr)\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda}\,.\end{split}

(7.13)

On the other hand, if
|x-y|^{2}\leqslant\frac{2}{N}
then

(\mathrm{d}_{1})=\int_{\lambda}^{\frac{N|x-y|^{2}}{2}}\mathrm{d}u\,\frac{1}{u}\mathrm{e}^{-u}\lesssim(\log N)\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda}\,,

(7.14)

which satisfies the bound (
7.2
). For the term
(\mathrm{d}_{2})
instead we have

\begin{split}(\mathrm{d}_{2})\lesssim\int_{1}^{\lambda^{3/4}}\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{c\lambda}{s}}\mathbbm{1}_{\lambda\geqslant 1}+\int_{\lambda^{3/4}\vee 1}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{e}^{-s^{1/3}}\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{c\lambda^{-1/4}}\,\end{split}

(7.15)

since
\lambda\geqslant|x-y|^{4}\tilde{g}(x)^{2}
. Putting (
7.13
), (
7.14
), and (
7.15
) together, we conclude the proof of (
7.2
).

We now prove (
7.3
). As above,

\begin{split}G(x,y)-G_{N}(x,y)&=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{e}^{-th}(x,y)-\int_{\frac{1}{N}}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{e}^{-th}(x,y)\\
&=\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{N}}\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{e}^{-t\kappa}\int\mathbb{W}_{x,y}^{t,0}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\,\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(\omega(s))}\,.\end{split}

Using Lemma
7.5
and assuming
|x|\geqslant|\tilde{x}|
, and
|x|\geqslant 1
, we obtain

\begin{split}\big|G(x,y)-G_{N}(x,y)\big|&\leqslant\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{N}}\mathrm{d}t\,\frac{1}{t}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{2t}}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{c}{2}t}\Bigl(\mathbbm{1}_{t\leqslant\tilde{g}(x)^{-2}|x|^{-2}}+\mathrm{e}^{-c\tilde{g}(x)t}\\
&\hskip 142.26378pt+\mathrm{e}^{-c|x|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)}}+\mathrm{e}^{-ct^{1/3}|x|^{2/3}\tilde{g}(x)^{2/3}}\Bigr)\,\\
&=\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}(\mathrm{a})+(\mathrm{b})+(\mathrm{c})+(\mathrm{d})\,.\end{split}

We begin with (a), and consider the cases
\tilde{g}(x)^{2}|x|^{2}>N
and
\tilde{g}(x)^{2}|x|^{2}\leqslant N
separately. For
\tilde{g}(x)^{2}|x|^{2}>N
we have a similar bound as in (
7.9
),

(\mathrm{a})\lesssim\int_{\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{2}\tilde{g}(x)^{2}|x|^{2}}^{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-s}\,.

If
|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)\leqslant 2
then we split the integral as

\begin{split}(a)\lesssim&\int_{\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{2}\tilde{g}(x)^{2}|x|^{2}}^{1}\,\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-s}+\int_{1}^{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-s}\\
\lesssim&\Bigl[\log(\tilde{g}(x)^{2}|x|^{2}|x-y|^{2})^{-1}\Bigr]_{+}+1\lesssim\Bigl[\log(N|x-y|^{2})^{-1}\Bigr]_{+}+1\,,\end{split}

using that
\tilde{g}(x)^{2}|x|^{2}>N
. On the other hand, if
|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)>2
, then
|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)>N|x-y|^{2}
. Thus we have

\int_{\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{2}\tilde{g}(x)^{2}|x|^{2}}^{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-s}\lesssim\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-c|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)^{2}|x|^{2}}}{|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)^{2}|x|^{2}}\lesssim\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-cN|x-y|^{2}}}{N|x-y|^{2}}\,.

Let us now consider when
\tilde{g}(x)^{2}|x|^{2}\leqslant N
, we perform a change of variable to get

\begin{split}(a)&\lesssim\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{N}}\mathrm{d}t\,\frac{1}{t}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{2t}}=\int_{\frac{N|x-y|^{2}}{2}}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-s}\\
&\lesssim\mathbbm{1}_{N|x-y|^{2}<2}\Bigl(\int_{\frac{N|x-y|^{2}}{2}}^{1}\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-s}+\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-s}\Bigr)+\mathbbm{1}_{N|x-y|^{2}>2}\int_{\frac{N|x-y|^{2}}{2}}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-s}\\
&\lesssim\Bigl(1+\Bigl[\log(N|x-y|^{2})^{-1}\Bigr]_{+}\Bigr)\wedge\,\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-cN|x-y|^{2}}}{N|x-y|^{2}}\,.\end{split}

(7.16)

Next, we consider the term (b). We proceed similarly as in (
7.12
) to obtain

\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{N}}\mathrm{d}t\,\frac{1}{t}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{2t}}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{c}{2}t-c\tilde{g}(x)t}\lesssim\int_{0}^{\frac{\tilde{g}(x)}{N}}\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)}{2s}}\mathrm{e}^{-s}\,.

If
\tilde{g}(x)\geqslant N
we split the term as

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)}{2s}}+\int_{1}^{\frac{\tilde{g}(x)}{N}}\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)}{2s}}\mathrm{e}^{-s}&\lesssim\int_{\frac{|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)}{2}}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\,\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-u}}{u}+\mathrm{e}^{-|x-y|^{2}N}\\
&\lesssim 1+\Bigl[\log(N|x-y|^{2})^{-1}\Bigr]_{+}+\mathrm{e}^{-|x-y|^{2}N}\,.\end{split}

Otherwise, if
\tilde{g}(x)<N
,

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{\frac{\tilde{g}(x)}{N}}\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{s}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}\tilde{g}(x)}{2s}}\mathrm{e}^{-s}&\lesssim\int_{\frac{|x-y|^{2}N}{2}}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\,\frac{1}{u}\mathrm{e}^{-u}\\
&\lesssim 1+\Bigl[\log(N|x-y|^{2})^{-1}\Bigr]_{+}\wedge\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-|x-y|^{2}N}}{N|x-y|^{2}}\,,\end{split}

where the last inequality is obtained by considering the cases
|x-y|^{2}<N^{-1}
and
|x-y|^{2}>N^{-1}
separately.

The terms (c) and (d) are treated similarly. This concludes the proof.
∎

To conclude this subsection, we estimate the function
\tau^{\varepsilon}
from (
2.17
).

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
d=2,3
and recall (
6.1
). Consider
U\geqslant 0
and
\tau^{\varepsilon}\equiv\tau^{\varepsilon,U}
defined as in (
2.17
). Then for any
\varepsilon>0
we have

\|\tau^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim_{\kappa,v}\chi(\varepsilon)\,.

Proof.

Using Proposition
7.1
, we get

\displaystyle\lvert\tau^{\varepsilon}(x)\rvert

\displaystyle\leqslant\int\mathrm{d}y\,\lvert v^{\varepsilon}(x-y)\rvert G(x,y)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{v}\,\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d}}\int_{\lvert z\rvert<\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}z\,\Bigl(\log\lvert z\rvert^{-1}\wedge 1\cdot\mathbbold{1}_{d=2}+\lvert z\rvert^{-1}\mathbbold{1}_{d=3}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\lesssim\,\chi(\varepsilon)\,.\qed

7.2.  Proof of Proposition
7.3

The key estimate behind the proof of Proposition
7.3
is the following bound on the gradient of the expectation of the Brownian bridge.

Lemma 7.7
.

Let
U
satisfy Assumption
2.1
and
d\geqslant 2
. Then, for almost every
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\Big|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(X_{s})}\Bigr]\Big|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\,,

where
\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}
denotes the expectation with respect to the law of the Brownian bridge measure
\mathbb{P}_{y,x}^{t,0}
from Section
5.2
, between
x
and
y
over time
t
.

Proof.

Suppose first that
U
is replaced with a smooth potential
V\vcentcolon\mathbb{R}^{d}\to[0,\infty)
. The Brownian bridge
(X_{s})_{s\in[0,t]}
going from
x
to
y
in time
t
can be written in terms of standard Brownian motion
(B_{s})_{s\geqslant 0}
as

X_{s}=B_{s}-\frac{s}{t}B_{t}+x+\frac{s}{t}(y-x)\,.

(7.17)

We denote the expectation with respect to Brownian motion
(B_{s})_{s\geqslant 0}
by
\mathbb{E}[\,\cdot\,]
. Thus,

\begin{split}&\nabla_{x}\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,V(X_{s})}\Bigr]\\
&\;=\nabla_{x}\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,V(B_{s}-\frac{s}{t}B_{t}+x+\frac{s}{t}(y-x))}\Bigr]\\
&\;=-\mathbb{E}\biggl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}r\,V(B_{r}-\frac{r}{t}B_{t}+x+\frac{r}{t}(y-x))}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,\biggl(1-\frac{s}{t}\biggr)\nabla V\biggl(B_{s}-\frac{s}{t}B_{t}+x+\frac{s}{t}(y-x)\biggr)\biggr]\\
&\;=-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,\biggl(1-\frac{s}{t}\biggr)\mathbb{E}\biggl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}r\,V(B_{r}-\frac{r}{t}B_{t}+x+\frac{r}{t}(y-x))}\nabla V\biggl(B_{s}-\frac{s}{t}B_{t}+x+\frac{s}{t}(y-x)\biggr)\biggr]\,.\end{split}

Therefore,

\nabla_{x}\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,V(X_{s})}\Bigr]=-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,\biggl(1-\frac{s}{t}\biggr)\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}r\,V(X_{r})}\nabla V(X_{s})\Bigr]\,.

(7.18)

We focus on the expectation inside the integral, conditioning on the value of
X_{s}
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}r\,V(X_{r})}\nabla V(X_{s})\Bigr]\\
&\quad=\int\mathrm{d}w\,\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}r\,V(X_{r})}\delta(X_{s}-w)\Bigr]\nabla V(w)\\
&\quad=\int\mathrm{d}w\,\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{s}\mathrm{d}r\,V(X_{r})}\delta(X_{s}-w)\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{s}^{t}\mathrm{d}r\,V(X_{r})}\Bigr]\nabla V(w)\\
&\quad=\int\mathrm{d}w\,\frac{\psi^{s}(x-w)\psi^{t-s}(w-y)}{\psi^{t}(x-y)}\mathbb{E}_{w,x}^{s,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{s}\mathrm{d}r\,V(X_{r})}\Bigr]\mathbb{E}_{y,w}^{t,s}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{s}^{t}\mathrm{d}r\,V(X_{r})}\Bigr]\nabla V(w)\,,\end{split}

(7.19)

where in the last equality we used the Markov property of Brownian motion to rewrite the probability measure of the Brownian bridge.

Let us first consider
U
satisfying
(D)
in Assumption
2.1
. Let
g
be the lower bound on
U
and
0<\gamma<1
given by Assumption
2.1
. Then (
7.19
) holds for
V=U
. Using the heat kernel bounds Lemma
7.5
, we find

\displaystyle\biggl\lvert\Bigl(1-\frac{s}{t}\Bigr)\mathbb{E}_{y,w}^{t,s}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{s}^{t}\mathrm{d}r\,U(X_{r})}\Bigr]\nabla U(w)\biggr\rvert

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{t-s}{t}\mathrm{e}^{-ct}\Bigl(\mathbbm{1}_{t-s\leqslant(g(\gamma y)\vee g(\gamma w))^{-2}(|y|+|w|)^{-2}}+\mathrm{e}^{-c(g(\gamma y)\vee g(\gamma w))(t-s)}+\mathrm{e}^{-c(|y|+|w|)\sqrt{g(\gamma y)\vee g(\gamma w)}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\mathrm{e}^{-c(t-s)^{1/3}(|y|+|w|)^{2/3}(g(\gamma y)\vee g(\gamma w))^{2/3}}\Bigr)\lvert\nabla U(w)\rvert

(7.20)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-ct}}{t}\lvert\nabla U(w)\rvert\Biggl(\frac{1}{g(\gamma w)^{2}\lvert w\rvert^{2}}+\frac{(t-s)}{(t-s)^{3/2}g(\gamma w)^{3/2}}+\frac{(t-s)}{g(\gamma w)^{2}\lvert w\rvert}+\frac{(t-s)}{(t-s)g(\gamma w)^{2}\lvert w\rvert^{2}}\Biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{1}{t\sqrt{t-s}}

for some positive constant
c\equiv c_{U}>0
and for all
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Thus,

\Bigl\lvert\nabla_{x}\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\Bigr[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(X_{s})}\Bigr]\Bigr\rvert\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,\frac{1}{t\sqrt{t-s}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\,,

as desired.

Next, suppose now that
U
satisfies
(P)
in Assumption
2.1
for some
\theta\geqslant 0
. Consider
V\equiv V_{\delta}=U*\varphi_{\delta}
where
\{\varphi_{\delta}\}_{\delta>0}
is a family of mollifiers such that
\varphi_{\delta}
has support inside the ball of radius
\delta>0
. Notice that
V_{\delta}
is smooth, and for any
\delta<1
,
V_{\delta}
satisfies
(P)
for the same power
\theta
. Moreover,

\lvert\nabla V_{\delta}(x)\rvert\leqslant\int\mathrm{d}y\,U(y)\lvert\nabla\varphi_{\delta}(x-y)\rvert\lesssim\frac{1}{\delta^{d+1}}(1+\lvert x\rvert^{\theta}).

Thus, using Lemma
7.5
with
g\vcentcolon x\mapsto 1+\lvert x\rvert^{\theta}
, (
7.18
), (
7.20
) and the fact that
\lvert\nabla V_{\delta}(x)\rvert/g(\gamma x)\lesssim_{\gamma}\delta^{-d-1}
for any
\gamma>0
,

\Bigl\lvert\nabla_{x}\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,V_{\delta}(X_{s})}\Bigr]\Bigr\rvert\lesssim_{U}\frac{1}{\delta^{d+1}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,\Bigl(1-\frac{s}{t}\Bigr)\frac{1}{(t-s)}\lesssim\frac{1}{\delta^{d+1}}

(7.21)

is bounded uniformly in
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
t>0
. We integrate by parts in (
7.19
), which yields

\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}r\,V_{\delta}(X_{r})}\nabla V_{\delta}(X_{s})\Bigr]\\
=-\int\mathrm{d}w\,V_{\delta}(w)\biggl(\Psi(w)\,\mathbb{E}_{w,x}^{s,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{s}\mathrm{d}r\,V_{\delta}(X_{r})}\Bigr]\mathbb{E}_{y,w}^{t,s}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{s}^{t}\mathrm{d}r\,V_{\delta}(X_{r})}\Bigr]\qquad\\
+\frac{\psi^{s}(x-w)\psi^{t-s}(w-y)}{\psi^{t}(x-y)}\,\mathbb{E}_{y,w}^{t,s}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{s}^{t}\mathrm{d}r\,V_{\delta}(X_{r})}\Bigr]\nabla_{x}\mathbb{E}_{w,x}^{s,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{s}\mathrm{d}r\,V_{\delta}(X_{r})}\Bigr]\biggr)\,,

where we defined

\Psi(w)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\nabla_{x}\biggl(\frac{\psi^{s}(x-w)\psi^{t-s}(w-y)}{\psi^{t}(x-y)}\biggr)=\biggl(\frac{x-y}{t}-\frac{x-w}{s}\biggr)\frac{\psi^{s}(x-w)\psi^{t-s}(w-y)}{\psi^{t}(x-y)}\,.

Introducing the notations

\displaystyle f_{\delta}(t,x,y)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\nabla_{x}\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,V_{\delta}(X_{s})}\bigr]\,,

\displaystyle f_{\delta}(t)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\sup\limits_{x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|f_{\delta}(t,x,y)|\,,

we have

f_{\delta}(t,x,y)=\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,\Bigl(1-\frac{s}{t}\Bigr)\int\mathrm{d}w\,V_{\delta}(w)\Psi(w)\,\mathbb{E}_{w,x}^{s,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{s}\mathrm{d}r\,V_{\delta}(X_{r})}\Bigr]\mathbb{E}_{y,w}^{t,s}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{s}^{t}\mathrm{d}r\,V_{\delta}(X_{r})}\Bigr]\\
+\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,\Bigl(1-\frac{s}{t}\Bigr)\int\mathrm{d}w\,V_{\delta}(w)\frac{\psi^{s}(x-w)\psi^{t-s}(w-y)}{\psi^{t}(x-y)}\mathbb{E}_{y,w}^{t,s}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{s}^{t}\mathrm{d}r\,V_{\delta}(X_{r})}\Bigr]f_{\delta}(s,x,w)\,.

(7.22)

Define

\displaystyle\alpha(s)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\Bigl(1-\frac{s}{t}\Bigr)\sup_{x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg|\int\mathrm{d}w\,V_{\delta}(w)\Psi(w)\,\mathbb{E}_{w,x}^{s,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{s}\mathrm{d}r\,V_{\delta}(X_{r})}\Bigr]\mathbb{E}_{y,w}^{t,s}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{s}^{t}\mathrm{d}r\,V_{\delta}(X_{r})}\Bigr]\bigg|\,;

\displaystyle\beta(s)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\Bigl(1-\frac{s}{t}\Bigr)\sup\limits_{x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg|\int\mathrm{d}w\,V_{\delta}(w)\frac{\psi^{s}(x-w)\psi^{t-s}(w-y)}{\psi^{t}(x-y)}\mathbb{E}_{y,w}^{t,s}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{s}^{t}\mathrm{d}r\,V_{\delta}(X_{r})}\Bigr]\bigg|\,.

To estimate
\alpha(s)
and
\beta(s)
, we use Hölder’s inequality, the fact that

\Big\|V_{\delta}(\,\cdot\,)\mathbb{E}_{y,\,\cdot\,}^{t,s}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{s}^{t}\mathrm{d}r\,V_{\delta}(X_{r})}\Bigr]\Big\|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{1}{t-s}

and

\int\mathrm{d}w\,\bigg|\frac{x-y}{t}-\frac{x-w}{s}\bigg|\frac{\psi^{s}(x-w)\psi^{t-s}(y-w)}{\psi^{t}(x-y)}=\frac{1}{s}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{W}}\bigl[|\mathcal{W}-\mu|\bigr]\,\lesssim\frac{\sigma}{s}\,,

(7.23)

where
\mathcal{W}
is a Gaussian random variable with law
\mathcal{N}(\mu,\sigma^{2}I_{d})
, with
\mu=x+\frac{s}{t}(y-x)
and
\sigma^{2}=\frac{s(t-s)}{t}
.
Thus, we can bound

\alpha(s)\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{t-s}}{t^{3/2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{s}},\quad\beta(s)\lesssim\frac{1}{t}.

Using (
7.21
) and (
7.22
), we find

f_{\delta}(t)\leqslant\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,\alpha(s)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,\beta(s)f_{\delta}(s)\,<\infty\,.

We can thus use Grönwall’s inequality to bound

f_{\delta}(t)\leqslant\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,\alpha(s)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,\Bigl(\int_{0}^{s}\mathrm{d}r\,\alpha(r)\Bigr)\beta(s)\exp\biggl(\int_{s}^{t}\mathrm{d}r\,\beta(r)\biggr)\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\,.

(7.24)

This estimate holds for any fixed
t>0
uniformly in
\delta>0
. The rest of the proof consists in controlling the limit
\delta\to 0
.

We claim that for any
\varepsilon>0

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathbb{P}_{y,x}^{t,0}\biggl(\biggl\lvert\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,\bigl(U(X_{s})-V_{\delta}(X_{s})\bigr)\biggr\rvert>\varepsilon\biggr)=0

(7.25)

To prove (
7.25
), we choose
\eta>0
and then
R_{\eta}>0
such that

\mathbb{P}_{y,x}^{t,0}(E_{R_{\eta}})\geqslant 1-\eta\,,\qquad E_{R}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\Bigl\{\max_{0\leqslant s\leqslant t}\lvert X_{s}\rvert\leqslant R\Bigr\}\,.

We can thus bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{y,x}^{t,0}\biggl(\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{\penalty\ d}s\,\bigl(U\bigl(X_{s}\bigr)-V_{\delta}\bigl(X_{s}\bigr)\bigr)\bigg|>\varepsilon\biggr)

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{P}_{y,x}^{t,0}\biggl(\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{\penalty\ d}s\,\big|U\bigl(X_{s}\bigr)-V_{\delta}\bigl(X_{s}\bigr)\big|>\varepsilon\biggr)

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{P}_{y,x}^{t,0}\biggl(\biggl\{\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{\penalty\ d}s\,\big|U\bigl(X_{s}\bigr)-V_{\delta}\bigl(X_{s}\bigr)\big|>\varepsilon\biggr\}\cap E_{R_{\eta}}\biggr)+\mathbb{P}_{y,x}^{t,0}\bigl(E_{R_{\eta}}^{c}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{\penalty\ d}s\,\big|U\bigl(X_{s}\bigr)-V_{\delta}\bigl(X_{s}\bigr)\big|\mathbbold{1}_{|X_{s}|\leqslant R_{\eta}}\biggr]+\eta\,,

where we used the Markov inequality and
E_{R_{\eta}}\subset\{\lvert X_{s}\rvert\leqslant R_{\eta}\}
for any
0\leqslant s\leqslant t
.
We can now estimate

\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\biggl[\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,\big|U(X_{s})-V_{\delta}(X_{s})\big|\mathbbold{1}_{\lvert X_{s}\rvert\leqslant R_{\eta}}\biggr]\leqslant\int_{\nu}^{t-\nu}\mathrm{d}s\,\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\Bigl[\big|U(X_{s})-V_{\delta}(X_{s})\big|\mathbbold{1}_{\lvert X_{s}\rvert\leqslant R_{\eta}}\Bigr]+C_{R_{\eta}}\nu

(7.26)

for any
0<\nu<t/2
using the fact that
U,V_{\delta}
are locally bounded. To bound the first term on the right-hand side of (
7.26
), we use the fact that
\nu\leqslant s\leqslant t-\nu
and thus

\displaystyle\int_{\nu}^{t-\nu}\mathrm{d}s\,\mathbb{E}^{t,0}_{y,x}\Bigl[\big|U(X_{s})-V_{\delta}(X_{s})\big|\mathbbold{1}_{\lvert X_{s}\rvert\leqslant R_{\eta}}\Bigr]

(7.27)

\displaystyle=\int_{\nu}^{t-\nu}\mathrm{d}s\int_{B_{R_{\eta}}}\mathrm{d}w\,\frac{\psi^{s}(x-w)\psi^{t-s}(y-w)}{\psi^{t}(x-y)}\lvert V_{\delta}(w)-U(w)\rvert

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{\nu,x,y,t}\int_{B_{R_{\eta}}}\mathrm{d}w\,\lvert V_{\delta}(w)-U(w)\rvert\,.

Since
V_{\delta}
converges to
U
in
L^{1}_{\operatorname{loc}}
, (
7.27
) tends to zero as
\delta\to 0
. Taking first
\delta\to 0
, then
\nu\to 0
, and then
\eta\to 0
, we therefore conclude (
7.25
).

From (
7.25
) and the Vitali convergence theorem, it follows that

\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(X_{s})}\Bigr]=\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,V_{\delta}(X_{s})}\Bigr]\,.

Hence, for any
x,y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\mspace{-10mu}\Bigr|\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(X_{s})}\Bigr]-\mathbb{E}_{y,z}^{t,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(X_{s})}\Bigr]\Bigl|

\displaystyle=\lim_{\delta\to 0}\Bigl\lvert\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,V_{\delta}(X_{s})}\Bigr]-\mathbb{E}_{y,z}^{t,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,V_{\delta}(X_{s})}\Bigr]\Bigr\rvert

\displaystyle=\lim_{\delta\to 0}\biggl\lvert\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\lambda\,{\nabla_{2}\mathbb{E}_{y,\lambda x+(1-\lambda)z}^{t,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,V_{\delta}(X_{s})}\Bigr]\cdot(x-z)}\biggr\rvert

\displaystyle\leqslant\limsup_{\delta\to 0}f_{\delta}(t)\cdot\lvert x-z\rvert\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\cdot\lvert x-z\rvert\,,

where we used (
7.24
).
Since
x\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(X_{s})}\bigr]
is
\frac{C}{\sqrt{t}}-
Lipschitz for some
C>0
, by Rademacher’s theorem it is almost everywhere differentiable and

\bigg|{\nabla_{x}\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(X_{s})}\Bigr]}\bigg|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}

for almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

∎

We are now ready to prove Proposition
7.3
.

Proof of Proposition
7.3
.

Using Lemma
5.11
, we write the kernel of the Green function
G
as

G(x,y)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa t}\psi^{t}(x-y)\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(X_{s})}\Bigr]\,.

Using the definition of
\psi^{t}(x)
as in (
5.32
), then we have

\begin{split}\nabla_{x}G(x,y)&=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa t}\frac{x-y}{t}\psi^{t}(x-y)\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\Bigr[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(X_{s})}\Bigl]\\
&+\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa t}\psi^{t}(x-y)\nabla_{x}\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(X_{s})}\Bigr]=\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}G_{1}+G_{2}\,.\end{split}

Following the proof of Proposition
7.1
, we now have

\lvert G_{1}\rvert\lesssim|x-y|^{-d+1}\mathrm{e}^{-c|x-y|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)\vee\tilde{g}(y)}}

for some
c>0
.

We can estimate
G_{2}
similarly. Indeed, from (
7.23
), Lemmas
7.7
and
7.5
, we get

\displaystyle\bigg|\nabla_{x}\mathbb{E}_{y,x}^{t,0}\Bigr[

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(X_{s})}\Bigl]\bigg|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\int\mathrm{d}w\,\nabla_{x}\bigg(\frac{\psi^{t/2}(x-w)\psi^{t/2}(w-y)}{\psi^{t}(x-y)}\bigg)\mathbb{E}_{y,w}^{t,t/2}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{t/2}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(X_{s})}\Bigr]\,\mathbb{E}_{w,x}^{t/2,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t/2}\mathrm{d}s\,U(X_{s})}\Bigl]\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg|\int\mathrm{d}w\,\frac{\psi^{t/2}(x-w)\psi^{t/2}(w-y)}{\psi^{t}(x-y)}\mathbb{E}_{y,w}^{t,t/2}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{t/2}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(X_{s})}\Bigr]\,\nabla_{x}\mathbb{E}_{w,x}^{t/2,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t/2}\mathrm{d}s\,U(X_{s})}\Bigl]\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\sup_{w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{y,w}^{t,t/2}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{t/2}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(X_{s})}\Bigr]\bigg(\sup_{w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{w,x}^{t/2,0}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t/2}\mathrm{d}s\,U(X_{s})}\Bigr]+1\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\mathrm{e}^{-ct}\Bigl(\mathbbm{1}_{t-s\leqslant g(\gamma y)^{-2}|y|^{-2}}+\mathrm{e}^{-cg(\gamma y)(t-s)}+\mathrm{e}^{-c|y|\sqrt{g(\gamma y)}}+\mathrm{e}^{-c(t-s)^{1/3}|y|^{2/3}g(\gamma y)^{2/3}}\Bigr)\,.

We can thus estimate

\lvert G_{2}\rvert\lesssim\lvert x-y\rvert^{-d+1}\mathrm{e}^{-c|x-y|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(y)}}\,.

By combining the estimates on
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
, we find

\lvert\nabla_{x}G(x,y)\rvert\lesssim\lvert x-y\rvert^{-d+1}\mathrm{e}^{-c|x-y|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(y)}}\,.\qed

8. The counterterm problem

This section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
2.14
. In particular, we extend the result of
(
4
, Section 5)
by solving the counterterm problem, defined in (
2.19
), for a wider class of potentials.
An important requirement for the solutions of the counterterm problem constructed in this section is that they give rise to a well-defined
\phi^{4}_{2}

field theory. We prove that this holds for all bare potentials

\mathcal{U}
satisfying Assumption
2.1
, assuming the growth condition (
2.26
) on the lower bound
g
.

The main difference between the argument presented below and previous works lies in the use of heat kernel estimates (see Proposition
7.1
), which allow us to treat faster-growing potentials without relying solely on an increase of the chemical potential
\kappa
. In addition, our approach requires control of the gradient of solutions to (
2.19
), a property that is essential for constructing the associated field theory via the methods of Section
4
whenever
\mathcal{U}
grows radially faster than a polynomial.

The proof of Theorem
2.14
relies on showing that the function

\Phi(U)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}={\mathcal{U}}+(\tau^{\varepsilon}-\tau^{\varepsilon,{0}})+v^{\varepsilon}*(\varrho_{\nu}^{U}-\varrho_{\nu}^{0})

(8.1)

admits a fixed point in an appropriate Banach space which we shall define below.
To this end, for any
\vartheta\vcentcolon\mathbb{R}^{d}\to(0,\infty)
, we define the norm

f\in L^{\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\longmapsto\sup\limits_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\bigg|\frac{f(x)}{\vartheta(x)}\bigg|=\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}\|f\|_{\vartheta}\in(0,\infty]\,.

In the following, we shall consider two different norms and their associated Banach spaces. In particular, for
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
, we define the Banach space

\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\{\,f\in W^{\alpha,\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\vcentcolon\|f\|_{\mathcal{U},\alpha}<\infty\,\}\,,

with the norm

\|f\|_{\mathcal{U},\alpha}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\|f\|_{\mathcal{U}}+\alpha\|\nabla f\|_{{\tilde{g}}^{3/2}}\,,

and
\tilde{g}
is defined in (
4.1
). This choice allows us, when
\mathcal{U}
is differentiable, to control appropriately the gradient of the solution of (
2.19
).
Consider

B_{\vartheta,r}({\mathcal{\vartheta}})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\Bigl\{f\in L^{\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\vcentcolon\|f-{\mathcal{\vartheta}}\|_{\mathcal{\vartheta}}\leqslant r\Bigr\}

the ball of radius
r>0
around
\vartheta
with respect to the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\vartheta}
. We set

B_{r}^{0}(\mathcal{U)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=B_{\mathcal{U},r}(\mathcal{U}),

B_{r}^{1}(\mathcal{U})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\Bigl\{f\in W^{1,\infty}_{\text{loc}}\vcentcolon f\in B_{\mathcal{U},r}(\mathcal{U}),\nabla f\in B_{{\tilde{g}}^{3/2},\tilde{r}}\bigl({{\tilde{g}}^{3/2}}\bigr)\Bigr\}\,,

where

\tilde{r}=\|\nabla\mathcal{U}\|_{\tilde{g}^{3/2}}+2\,.

Remark 8.1
.

Consider
r\in(0,1)
. If
u\in B_{\mathcal{U},r}({\mathcal{U}})
, then

(1-r){\mathcal{U}}(x)\leqslant u(x)\leqslant(1+r){\mathcal{U}}(x)\quad\text{a.s.}

Hence, we get that
u
is a positive function and

\tr{(h^{u})^{-2}}\leqslant(1-r)^{-1}\tr{(h^{\mathcal{U}})^{-2}}<\infty\,.

Finally, we observe that if
g\lesssim\mathcal{U}
, then
g\lesssim_{r}u
.

For
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
,
B^{\alpha}_{r}(\mathcal{U})
are complete metric spaces, where the metric is the one induced by the norm on
\mathcal{B}_{\alpha}
. The existence and uniqueness of solutions to the counterterm problem (for fixed
\varepsilon,\nu>0
) follows by showing that the map
\Phi
satisfies, for any
r\in(0,1)
,
\Phi(B_{r}^{\alpha}({\mathcal{U}}))\subset B_{r}^{\alpha}({\mathcal{U}})
, and it is a contraction on
B^{\alpha}_{r}(\mathcal{U})
, see
(
4
, Theorem 5.2)
.

In the following arguments, (
2.26
) will allow us to control the behaviour of
\mathcal{U}
in a neighbourhood of a given point
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
using (
2.27
), as we can then estimate

\esssup_{\lvert x-y\rvert\leqslant 1}\mathcal{U}(x)\lesssim_{c}g^{3/2}\bigl((\gamma+c)x\bigr)

(8.2)

for any
c>0
.

Lemma 8.2
.

Let
r\in(0,1)
, and
u,u_{1},u_{2}\in B_{r}^{\alpha}({\mathcal{U}})
be positive functions. Then, uniformly in
\varepsilon>0
small enough and for
\kappa>1
, there exists
C>0
such that,

(i)

\lvert(\tau^{\varepsilon,u}-\tau^{\varepsilon,0})(x)\rvert\leqslant\frac{C}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}\,g(x)\,,

(ii)

\lvert(\tau^{\varepsilon,u_{1}}-\tau^{\varepsilon,u_{2}})(x)\rvert\leqslant\frac{C}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{\mathcal{U}}\,g(x)\,,

(iii)

if
\mathcal{U}
satisfies
(D)
,

\lvert\nabla\tau^{\varepsilon,u}(x)\rvert\leqslant\frac{C}{{\kappa}^{1/4}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}\,{\tilde{g}}^{3/2}(x)

and

\lvert\nabla(\tau^{\varepsilon,u_{1}}-\tau^{\varepsilon,u_{2}})(x)\rvert\leqslant\frac{C}{{\kappa}^{1/4}}\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{\mathcal{U}}\,{\tilde{g}}^{3/2}(x)\,.

Proof.

The strategy is analogous to
(
4
, Theorem 5.2)
. Let
0<r<1
and
u\in B_{\mathcal{U},r}({\mathcal{U}})
.
By applying a resolvent expansion and using the bound
u(y)\leqslant\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}{\mathcal{U}}(y)
, we find

\displaystyle\lvert(\tau^{\varepsilon,u}-\tau^{\varepsilon,0})(x)\rvert

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\,v^{\varepsilon}(x-\tilde{x})\int\mathrm{d}y\,\frac{1}{h^{u}}(x,y)u(y)\frac{1}{h^{0}}(y,\tilde{x})

\displaystyle\leqslant\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\,v^{\varepsilon}(x-\tilde{x})\int\mathrm{d}y\,\frac{1}{h^{u}}(x,y){\mathcal{U}}(y)\frac{1}{h^{0}}(y,\tilde{x})

(8.3)

\displaystyle=\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\,v^{\varepsilon}(x-\tilde{x})\int_{\lvert x-y\rvert\leqslant 1}\mathrm{d}y\,\frac{1}{h^{u}}(x,y){\mathcal{U}}(y)\frac{1}{h^{0}}(y,\tilde{x})

(8.4)

\displaystyle\quad+\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\,v^{\varepsilon}(x-\tilde{x})\int_{\lvert x-y\rvert>1}\mathrm{d}y\,\frac{1}{h^{u}}(x,y){\mathcal{U}}(y)\frac{1}{h^{0}}(y,\tilde{x})\,.

(8.5)

Let us first bound (
8.4
). We can estimate

(
8.4
)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}\esssup_{\lvert z-x\rvert\leqslant 1}{\mathcal{U}}(z)\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\,v^{\varepsilon}(x-\tilde{x})\int_{\lvert x-y\rvert\leqslant 1}\mathrm{d}y\,\frac{1}{h^{u}}(x,y)\frac{1}{h^{0}}(y,\tilde{x})

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\leqslant C\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}\esssup_{\lvert z-x\rvert\leqslant 1}{\mathcal{U}}(z)\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\,v^{\varepsilon}(x-\tilde{x})\int_{\lvert x-y\rvert\leqslant 1}\mathrm{d}y\,\Bigl(\frac{1}{h^{u}}(x,y)\frac{1}{h^{0}}(y,x)+\frac{1}{h^{u}}(\tilde{x},y)\frac{1}{h^{0}}(y,\tilde{x})\Bigr)\end{split}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{\tilde{\gamma}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}\sup_{\lvert\tilde{x}-x\rvert<\varepsilon}\frac{\log(g(x))}{(\kappa+g(\tilde{\gamma}\tilde{x}))}\esssup_{\lvert z-x\rvert\leqslant 1}{\mathcal{U}}(z)\,,

(8.6)

for any fixed
0<\tilde{\gamma}<1
, where we used
\widehat{v^{\varepsilon}}(0)=1
and Proposition
7.1
in the last inequality. From Assumption
2.11
and (
8.2
), by choosing
\tilde{\gamma}=\sqrt{\gamma+c}
, and using the fact that, since
g
is radially non-decreasing,

g\bigl((1-\varepsilon)x\bigr)\lesssim\inf\limits_{\lvert x-\tilde{x}\rvert<\varepsilon}g(\tilde{x})\,,

we find

\displaystyle\eqref{Bound of first part taueps0}\leqslant C\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}\frac{g\bigl((\gamma+c)x\bigr)^{3/2}}{\bigl(\kappa+g(\tilde{\gamma}^{2}x)\bigr)}\leqslant\frac{C}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}g\bigl((\gamma+c)x\bigr)\,,

(8.7)

for all
0<c<1-\gamma
and
\varepsilon>0
small enough.
We now consider (
8.5
). Using Proposition
7.1
with the assumption that
\kappa>1
, we find

(
8.5
)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\,v^{\varepsilon}(x-\tilde{x})\int_{|x-y|>1}\mathrm{d}y\,\frac{1}{h^{u}}(x,y){\mathcal{U}}(y)\frac{1}{h^{0}}(y,\tilde{x})

\displaystyle\begin{split}{}&\leqslant C\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\,v^{\varepsilon}(x-\tilde{x})\int_{|x-y|>1}\mathrm{d}y\,\,\mathrm{e}^{-c\sqrt{\kappa+g(\tilde{\gamma}x)\vee g(\tilde{\gamma}y)}|x-y|}{\mathcal{U}}(y)\mathrm{e}^{-\sqrt{\kappa}\lvert\tilde{x}-y\rvert}\end{split}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}\,,

(8.8)

where the last step follows from (
2.27
).
From Assumption
2.11
, (
8.7
) and (
8.8
), we have

\eqref{eq:taueps diff}\leqslant\frac{C}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}g((\gamma+c)x)\,.

The bound in
(i)
is then a consequence of the fact that
0<\gamma<1
and thus
g\bigl((\gamma+c)x\bigr)\leqslant g(x)
for any
c>0
small enough. Starting with a resolvent expansion and following the same steps as above,
(ii)
follows.

Let us now consider
(iii)
. Writing
\nabla_{x}\tau^{\varepsilon,u}=\nabla_{x}({\tau^{\varepsilon,u}-\tau^{\varepsilon,0}})
, using a resolvent expansion and integration by parts, we find

\lvert\nabla_{x}\tau^{\varepsilon,u}(x)\rvert\leqslant\lvert\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\,v^{\varepsilon}(x-\tilde{x})\int\mathrm{d}y\,\nabla_{x}\frac{1}{h^{u}}(x,y)U(y)\frac{1}{h^{0}}(y,\tilde{x})\rvert\\
+\lvert\int\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\,v^{\varepsilon}(x-\tilde{x})\int\mathrm{d}y\,\frac{1}{h^{u}}(x,y)U(y)\nabla_{\tilde{x}}\frac{1}{h^{0}}(y,\tilde{x})\rvert\,.

(8.9)

We show the bound of the first term (
8.9
), as the second one can be estimated in a similar way.

Using Proposition
7.3
, and splitting cases as in step (i), by using

\displaystyle\lvert\nabla_{x}\frac{1}{h^{u}}(x,y)\rvert\leqslant\frac{C\mathrm{e}^{-c\lvert x-y\rvert\sqrt{\kappa+g(\tilde{\gamma}y)}}}{\lvert x-y\rvert}\,,

for any
\gamma<\tilde{\gamma}<1
, we find

\lvert\nabla_{x}\tau^{\varepsilon,u}(x)\rvert\leqslant\frac{C\log(g(\tilde{\gamma}x))}{\sqrt{\kappa+g(\tilde{\gamma}^{2}x)}}g\bigl((\gamma+c)x\bigr)^{3/2}\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}\leqslant\frac{C}{\kappa^{1/4}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}\tilde{g}^{3/2}(x)\,,

for
c>0
small enough.
Notice that, to show the last inequality, we used (
2.26
).
We can show similarly that

\lvert\nabla(\tau^{\varepsilon,u_{1}}-\tau^{\varepsilon,u_{2}})(x)\rvert\leqslant\frac{C}{\kappa^{1/4}}\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{\mathcal{U}}\,{\tilde{g}}^{3/2}(x)\,.\qed

For
\nu>0
, we define the quantum Green function

\mathcal{G}_{\nu}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\frac{\nu}{\mathrm{e}^{\nu h}-1}=\nu\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-\nu nh}.

(8.10)

Note that the mean particle density (
2.15
) satisfies

\varrho_{\nu}(x)=\mathcal{G}_{\nu}(x,x).

(8.11)

In order to show the corresponding result of Lemma
8.2
for the difference of mean particle densities, we establish the following bounds for the quantum Green function, equivalent to Proposition
7.1
.

Proposition 8.3
.

Let
d\geqslant 2
and
\mathcal{G}_{\nu}
be the quantum Green function as in (
8.10
).

(i)

Under the same assumptions as Proposition
7.1
, we have the following bound on its kernel

\mathcal{G}_{\nu}(x,y)\lesssim\Bigl[\Bigl(\bigl[\log\bigl(|x-y|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)\vee\tilde{g}(y)}\bigr)^{-1}\bigr]_{+}\wedge\log\nu^{-1}\Bigr)\vee 1\Bigr]\mathrm{e}^{-c|x-y|\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)\vee\tilde{g}(y)}}

in dimension
d=2
, and

\mathcal{G}_{\nu}(x,y)\lesssim\frac{1}{\nu^{d-2}\vee\lvert x-y\rvert^{d-2}}\mathrm{e}^{-c\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)\vee\tilde{g}(y)}\lvert x-y\rvert}

when
d\geqslant 3
.

(ii)

Furthermore, under the same assumptions as Proposition
7.3
, we bound the gradient of the kernel of the quantum Green function by

\lvert\nabla_{x}\mathcal{G}_{\nu}(x,y)\rvert\lesssim\frac{1}{\nu^{d+1}\vee\lvert x-y\rvert^{d+1}}\mathrm{e}^{-c\sqrt{\tilde{g}(y)}\lvert x-y\rvert}\,.

Proof.

Point
(i)
can be shown using the heat kernel estimates in Lemma
7.5
,

\mathcal{G}_{\nu}(x,y)\lesssim\nu\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\,\frac{1}{(n\nu)^{d/2}}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{2n\nu}}\mathrm{e}^{-c\frac{n\nu}{2}}\Bigl(\mathbbm{1}_{n\nu\leqslant(\tilde{g}(x)\vee\tilde{g}(y))^{-2}(|x|+|\tilde{x}|)^{-2}}+\mathrm{e}^{-c(\tilde{g}(x)\vee\tilde{g}(y))n\nu}\\
\hskip 85.35826pt+\mathrm{e}^{-c(|x|+|\tilde{x}|)\sqrt{\tilde{g}(x)\vee\tilde{g}(y)}}+\mathrm{e}^{-c{(n\nu)}^{1/3}(|x|+|\tilde{x}|)^{2/3}(\tilde{g}(x)\vee\tilde{g}(y))^{2/3}}\Bigr)\,,

and noticing that every term of the sum is of the form

\nu\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}f(\nu n)
,
where the function
f
has a global maximum at
x_{0}\in(0,\infty)
, is increasing in
[0,x_{0}]
, and decreasing in
[x_{0},\infty)
. We can thus bound

\nu\sum_{n=1}^{\lfloor x_{0}/\nu\rfloor}f(\nu n)\lesssim\int_{\nu}^{x_{0}}\mathrm{d}t\,f(t)

and

\nu\sum_{{\lceil x_{0}/\nu\rceil}}^{\infty}f(\nu n)\lesssim\nu f(x_{0})+\int_{\lceil x_{0}/\nu\rceil\nu}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\,f(t)\,.

By proceeding as in the proof of Proposition
7.1
with the change of variables
\nu=1/N
and considering the cases
\nu\leqslant x_{0}
and
\nu\geqslant x_{0}
separately, we conclude the proof.

The proof of
(ii)
is done in a similar way. We start by noting that, following the proof of Proposition
7.3
,

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}(\mathrm{e}^{-th})_{x,y}

\displaystyle=\ \mathrm{e}^{-\kappa t}\frac{x-y}{t}\psi^{t}(x-y)\mathbb{E}_{x,y}^{t}\Bigr[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(X_{s})}\Bigl]

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa t}\psi^{t}(x-y)\nabla_{x}\mathbb{E}_{x,y}^{t}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(X_{s})}\Bigr]\,

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa t}\frac{x-y}{t}\psi^{t}(x-y)\mathbb{E}_{x,y}^{t}\Bigr[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(X_{s})}\Bigl]

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\mathrm{e}^{-\kappa t}\psi^{t}(x-y)\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\sup_{w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{w,y}^{t/2}\Bigl[\mathrm{e}^{-\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\,U(X_{s})}\Bigr]\,.

We can now conclude using heat kernel estimates, and arguing that the Riemann sums are bounded as above by the corresponding integral.
∎

Remark 8.4
.

From a similar argument to Proposition
8.3
, it follows that, uniformly in
0\leqslant t<1
,

\Bigl(\frac{\nu\mathrm{e}^{t\nu h}}{\mathrm{e}^{\nu h}-1}\Bigr)(x,y)

is bounded by the left-hand side of (
7.2
), and a similar result holds for its gradient.

Lemma 8.5
.

Let
r\in(0,1)
, and
u,u_{1},u_{2}\in B_{r}^{\alpha}({\mathcal{U}})
be positive functions. Then, uniformly for
\varepsilon>0
small enough and for
\kappa>1
, there exists
C>0
such that,

(i)

\big|v^{\varepsilon}*(\varrho_{\nu}^{u}-\varrho_{\nu}^{0})(x)\big|\leqslant\frac{C}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}\,g(x)

(ii)

\lvert v^{\varepsilon}*\bigl(\varrho_{\nu}^{u_{1}}-\varrho_{\nu}^{u_{2}}\bigr)(x)\rvert\leqslant\frac{C}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{\mathcal{U}}\,g(x)

(iii)

if
\mathcal{U}
satisfies
(D)
then

\big|\nabla\bigl(v^{\varepsilon}*\varrho_{\nu}^{u}\bigr)(x)\big|\leqslant\frac{C}{\kappa^{1/4}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}\,{\tilde{g}}^{3/2}(x)

and

\big|\nabla\bigl(v^{\varepsilon}*\bigl(\varrho_{\nu}^{u_{1}}-\varrho_{\nu}^{u_{2}}\bigr)\bigr)(x)\big|\leqslant\frac{C}{\kappa^{1/4}}\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{\mathcal{U}}\,{\tilde{g}}^{3/2}(x)\,.

Proof.

The proof strategy is the same as Lemma
8.2
.
Recalling (
8.11
) and applying a resolvent expansion, we get

\displaystyle-(\varrho_{\nu}^{u}-\varrho_{\nu}^{0})(y)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{G}_{\nu}^{0}(y,y)-\mathcal{G}_{\nu}^{u}(y,y)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,\int\mathrm{d}z\,\Bigl(\frac{\nu\mathrm{e}^{t\nu h^{u}}}{\mathrm{e}^{\nu h^{u}}-1}\Bigr)(y,z)u(z)\Bigl(\frac{\nu\mathrm{e}^{(1-t)\nu h^{0}}}{\mathrm{e}^{\nu h^{0}}-1}\Bigr)(z,y)\,.

(8.12)

To bound (
8.12
), we follow the same steps as in the proof of
(
4
, Theorem 5.2)
,
cf.
(5.16), obtaining

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:quantumgreenfunction_resolventexpansion}\leqslant\,

\displaystyle C\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}\esssup_{|z-y|<1}\mathcal{U}(z)\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,\int_{|z-y|<1}\mathrm{d}z\,\Bigl(\frac{\nu\mathrm{e}^{t\nu h^{u}}}{\mathrm{e}^{\nu h^{u}}-1}\Bigr)(y,z)\Bigl(\frac{\nu\mathrm{e}^{(1-t)\nu h^{0}}}{\mathrm{e}^{\nu h^{0}}-1}\Bigr)(z,y)

(8.13)

\displaystyle+C\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,\int_{|z-y|\geqslant 1}\mathrm{d}z\,\Bigl(\frac{\nu\mathrm{e}^{t\nu h^{u}}}{\mathrm{e}^{\nu h^{u}}-1}\Bigr)(y,z)\mathcal{U}(z)\Bigl(\frac{\nu\mathrm{e}^{(1-t)\nu h^{0}}}{\mathrm{e}^{\nu h^{0}}-1}\Bigr)(z,y)\Bigr)\,.

(8.14)

We can now refer to Lemma
8.2
for the bound of
\big|v^{\varepsilon}*(\varrho_{\nu}^{u}-\varrho_{\nu}^{0})(x)\big|
. In particular, by Remark
8.4
, the contribution from (
8.13
), resp. (
8.14
) can be estimated as in (
8.4
), resp. (
8.5
). This proves (i). A similar argument, together with an application of Proposition
8.3
, yields (ii) and (iii).
∎

Proposition 8.6
.

Let
r\in(0,1)
,
{\mathcal{U}}
satisfying Assumption
2.11
and let
\alpha=0
. Then, there exists a positive chemical potential
\kappa_{0}(r)>0
, such that the function
\Phi
defined in (
8.1
)
satisfies

\Phi(B_{r}^{\alpha}({\mathcal{U}}))\subset B_{r}^{\alpha}({\mathcal{U}})

and is a contraction, for all
\kappa>\kappa_{0}(r)
.

The same result holds for
\alpha=1
if
\mathcal{U}
satisfies
(D)
.

Proof.

Let
u\in B_{r}^{\alpha}({\mathcal{U}})
and
\alpha=0
. From parts (i) of Lemmas
8.2
and
8.5
, we have, for all
\kappa>1
,

\displaystyle\lvert(\tau^{\varepsilon,u}-\tau^{\varepsilon,{0}})(x)-v^{\varepsilon}*(\varrho_{\nu}^{u}-\varrho_{\nu}^{0})(x)\rvert

\displaystyle\leqslant C\kappa^{-1/2}\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}{g}(x)\,\leqslant C\kappa^{-1/2}\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}\,{\mathcal{U}}(x)\,.

We thus find, using the triangle inequality and
\|{\mathcal{U}}\|_{{\mathcal{U}}}=1

\|\Phi(u)-{\mathcal{U}}\|_{\mathcal{U}}\leqslant C\kappa^{-1/2}\|u-{\mathcal{U}}\|_{\mathcal{U}}+C\kappa^{-1/2}<r

(8.15)

where the second inequality is verified for
\kappa
big enough.
From parts (ii) of Lemmas
8.2
and
8.5
, we find similarly

\|\Phi(u_{1})-\Phi(u_{2})\|_{\mathcal{U}}\leqslant C\kappa^{-1/2}\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{\mathcal{U}}<\frac{1}{4}\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{\mathcal{U}}

(8.16)

for
\kappa
big enough.
We can now choose
\kappa_{0}(r)>0
big enough such that for all
\kappa>\kappa_{0}(r)
, (
8.15
) and (
8.16
) hold.

If
\mathcal{U}
satisfies
(D)
, we can further show that, for
\kappa>0
big enough,

\big\|\nabla\Phi(u)-{{\tilde{g}}^{3/2}}\big\|_{{\tilde{g}}^{3/2}}\leqslant\|\nabla\mathcal{U}\|_{\tilde{g}^{3/2}}+1+\frac{C}{\kappa^{1/4}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}}\leqslant\|\nabla\mathcal{U}\|_{\tilde{g}^{3/2}}+2\,.

In particular,
\Phi(u)\in B_{r}^{1}({\mathcal{U}})
. Moreover, parts (iii) of Lemmas
8.2
and
8.5
yield

\big\|\nabla\Phi(u_{1})-\nabla\Phi(u_{2})\big\|_{{\tilde{g}}^{3/2}}<\frac{1}{4}\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{\mathcal{U}}\,.

We then bound

\|\Phi(u_{1})-\Phi(u_{2})\|_{\mathcal{U},1}<\frac{1}{2}\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{\mathcal{U},0}<\frac{1}{2}\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{\mathcal{U},1}\,.\qed

An application of Banach fixed point theorem shows point (i) of Theorem
2.14
.

Corollary 8.7
.

In the hypotheses of Proposition
8.6
, for any fixed
\varepsilon,\nu>0
, there exists a unique positive solution
u^{\varepsilon}_{\nu}\in B^{\alpha}_{r}(\mathcal{U})
to (
2.19
).

In the next two lemmas, we show that the family of solutions
\{u_{\nu}^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon,\nu>0}
has a limit for
\varepsilon,\nu\to 0
and
\varepsilon
satisfies (
2.28
). Notice that, by completeness, the limit is in
B^{\alpha}_{r}(\mathcal{U})
.

Lemma 8.8
.

Let
r\in(0,1)
and consider, for every
\varepsilon,\nu>0
, the solution
u_{\nu}^{\varepsilon}\in B_{r}^{\alpha}({\mathcal{U}})
to (
2.19
), as per Corollary
8.7
. Then, uniformly in
\nu>0
, and for all
\kappa>0
big enough, the family
\{u_{\nu}^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon>0}
is Cauchy in
(B_{r}^{\alpha}({\mathcal{U}}),\|\,\cdot\,\|_{\mathcal{U},\alpha})
for
\varepsilon\to 0
.

Proof.

Let us first consider the case
\alpha=0
.
Let
\nu,\varepsilon^{\prime},\varepsilon>0
,
r\in(0,1)
and
u^{\varepsilon},u^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\in B_{r}^{\alpha}({\mathcal{U}})
solutions to (
2.19
) for fixed inverse temperature
\nu
and interaction range of respectively of
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}
. We have

u^{\varepsilon}(x)-u^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}(x)=(\tau^{\varepsilon,u^{\varepsilon}}(x)-\tau^{\varepsilon,{0}})-(\tau^{\varepsilon^{\prime},u^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}(x)-\tau^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}_{0})+v^{\varepsilon}*(\varrho_{\nu}^{u^{\varepsilon}}(x)-\varrho_{\nu}^{0})-v^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}*(\varrho_{\nu}^{u^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}(x)-\varrho_{\nu}^{0}).

(8.17)

We can show the result by treating the terms in pairs and bounding them by the difference between
u^{\varepsilon}
and
u^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}
and a vanishing
\varepsilon
-dependent part. We start by bounding

\big|(\tau^{\varepsilon,u^{\varepsilon}}(x)-\tau^{\varepsilon,{0}})-(\tau^{\varepsilon^{\prime},u^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}(x)-\tau^{\varepsilon^{\prime},0})\big|\\
\leqslant\big|\tau^{\varepsilon,u^{\varepsilon}}(x)-\tau^{\varepsilon,u^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}(x)\big|+\big|\tau^{\varepsilon,u^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}(x)-\tau^{\varepsilon,0}-(\tau^{\varepsilon^{\prime},u^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}(x)-\tau^{\varepsilon^{\prime},0})\big|=\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}I_{1}+I_{2}.

(8.18)

The term
I_{1}
is bounded using Lemma
8.2

(ii)

I_{1}\leqslant\frac{C}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\|u^{\varepsilon}-u^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\|_{\mathcal{U}}

and
I_{2}
vanishes with
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}\to 0
by dominated convergence, since

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg|\int\mathrm{d}y\,\bigl(v^{\varepsilon}(x-y)-v^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}(x-y)\bigr)(G^{u^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}(x,y)-G^{0}(x,y))\bigg|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\|u^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}\|_{\mathcal{U}}{\mathcal{U}}(x)\leqslant\frac{C}{\sqrt{\kappa}}(1+r){\mathcal{U}}(x)

uniformly in
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
as stated in Lemma
8.2

(i)
, and
v^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\delta
as
\varepsilon\to 0
.

The two other pairs of terms can be treated in the same way, since they are bounded uniformly in
\nu>0
for any fixed
\varepsilon>0
.

We can argue similarly if
\alpha=1
, whenever
\mathcal{U}
satisfies
(D)
, by bounding in a similar way the quantity

\nabla u^{\varepsilon}(x)-\nabla u^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}(x)=\nabla\tau^{\varepsilon,u^{\varepsilon}}(x)-{\nabla\tau^{\varepsilon^{\prime},u^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}}(x)+\nabla\bigl(v^{\varepsilon}*\varrho_{\nu}^{u^{\varepsilon}}\bigr)(x)-\nabla\bigl(v^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}*\varrho_{\nu}^{u^{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}\bigr)(x)\,.\qed

Lemma 8.9
.

Let
r\in(0,1)
,
\alpha\in\{0,1\}
,
u_{\nu}^{\varepsilon}\in B_{\alpha,r}({\mathcal{U}})
the solution to (
2.19
), for fixed
\varepsilon,\nu>0
and
\theta
as in Remark
2.3
. For
\varepsilon>0
satisfying (
2.28
), the family
\{u_{\nu}^{\varepsilon}\}_{\nu,\varepsilon>0}
is Cauchy in
(B_{r}^{\alpha}({\mathcal{U}}),\|\,\cdot\,\|_{\mathcal{U},\alpha})
for
\nu,\varepsilon\to 0
. As a consequence, there exists
U\in B_{r}^{\alpha}({\mathcal{U}})
such that

\lim_{\nu,\varepsilon\to 0}u^{\varepsilon}_{\nu}=U\,.

Proof.

Let us first consider the case where
\alpha=0
.
Using a triangle equality and (
8.8
), it is enough to bound appropriately the difference

u_{\nu}(x)-u_{\nu^{\prime}}(x)=(\tau^{\varepsilon,u_{\nu}}(x)-\tau^{\varepsilon,u_{\nu^{\prime}}}(x))+v^{\varepsilon}*\bigl[(\varrho_{\nu}^{u_{\nu}}(x)-\varrho_{\nu}^{0})-(\varrho_{{\nu^{\prime}}}^{u_{\nu^{\prime}}}(x)-\varrho_{\nu^{\prime}}^{0})\bigr]

(8.19)

where
\nu,\nu^{\prime},\varepsilon>0
,
0<r<1
and
u_{\nu},u_{\nu^{\prime}}\in B_{\mathcal{U},r}({\mathcal{U}})
solutions to (
2.19
) for fixed
\varepsilon
and inverse temperature of respectively
\nu,\nu^{\prime}
.
Using Lemma
8.2
(ii), Lemma
8.5
(ii) and (
8.19
), we find

(1-C\kappa^{-1/2})\|u_{\nu}-u_{\nu^{\prime}}\|_{\mathcal{U}}\leqslant\|v^{\varepsilon}*A\|_{\mathcal{U}}

where

A(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\biggl[\frac{\nu}{\mathrm{e}^{\nu(-\Delta/2+\kappa+u_{\nu})}-1}-\frac{\nu}{\mathrm{e}^{\nu(-\Delta/2+\kappa)}-1}\biggr](x,x)\\
-\biggl[\frac{\nu^{\prime}}{\mathrm{e}^{\nu^{\prime}(-\Delta/2+\kappa+u_{\nu})}-1}-\frac{\nu^{\prime}}{\mathrm{e}^{\nu^{\prime}(-\Delta/2+\kappa)}-1}\biggr](x,x)\,.

(8.20)

Using the arguments of
(
4
, Proof of Theorem 5.2, pp. 957-959)
, we can show

\|v^{\varepsilon}*A\|_{\mathcal{U}}\lesssim_{v}\frac{(\nu\vee\nu^{\prime})^{1-s/2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}

by noting the following quantitative estimate for
u_{\nu}\in B_{r}(\mathcal{U})

\Big\|\frac{\nu\mathrm{e}^{\nu th_{\nu}}}{\mathrm{e}^{\nu h_{\nu}}-1}-\frac{1}{h_{\nu}}\Big\|^{2}_{\mathfrak{S}^{2}}\lesssim\nu^{2-s}

and

\biggl[\frac{\nu\mathrm{e}^{\nu t(-\Delta+\kappa)}}{\mathrm{e}^{\nu(-\Delta+\kappa)}-1}-\frac{1}{(-\Delta+\kappa)}\biggr]^{2}(0,0)\lesssim\nu

uniform in
t\in[0,1)
, which we can obtain using the methods described in Section
6.1.1
.

The case
\alpha=1
follows in a similar way, with the estimate

\Biggl(1-\frac{C}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\Biggr)\|u_{\nu}-u_{\nu^{\prime}}\|_{\mathcal{U},1}\leqslant\|v^{\varepsilon}*A\|_{\mathcal{U}}+\|(\nabla v^{\varepsilon})*A\|_{\tilde{g}^{3/2}}\lesssim_{v}\frac{(\nu^{\prime}\vee\nu)^{1-s/2}}{\varepsilon^{3}}\,.\qed

Appendix A
Non-solvability of (
3.8
) (with Amirali Hannani)

In this section, we discuss (
3.8
). As stated previously, finding a bounded, integrable solution
\alpha^{\varepsilon}
to (
3.8
) is enough to show the convergence of the renormalised many-body system to the
\phi^{4}_{2}
field theory, using the methods described in
7
. We prove in this section that, for any potential
v
chosen as in Assumption
2.5
, such a solution cannot exist, and thus the methods presented in Section
6
are a necessary step to our proof.

The argument relies on the fact that the left hand-side of (
3.8
) is necessarily as regular as
v
, since it is a convolution, whilst the regularity of the right-hand side,
\tau^{\varepsilon}
, is a function of the smoothness of the potential
U
considered.

Let us now show that, for any positive potential
v
satisfying Assumption
2.5
, there is no solution
\alpha^{\varepsilon}
to (
3.8
). Notice that the same proof can be extended to general potentials
v
, by choosing an external potential
U
which is discontinuous at some point
x
where
\tau^{\varepsilon}(x)\neq 0
.

Proposition A.1
.

Let
v
be a positive interaction potential satisfying Assumption
2.5
, and let
U
be a positive locally bounded external potential that is not continuous. Then

u(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{.}\hbox{.}}}=\int\mathrm{d}y\,G^{U}(x,y)v^{\varepsilon}(x-y)

is not in
C^{2}
.

Proof.

Notice that

x\mapsto\int\mathrm{d}y\,G(x,y)g(x-y)

and

x\mapsto\int\mathrm{d}y\,\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}G(x,y)g(x-y)

are continuous for any smooth, bounded function
g
by Remark
7.4
and dominated convergence. Indeed, for any
y\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, the maps
x\mapsto G(x,y)
and
x\mapsto\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}G(x,y)
are continuous on the set
\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\{y\}
.

Let us now assume by contradiction that

u(x)=\int\mathrm{d}x\,G(x,y)v^{\varepsilon}(x-y)

is
C^{2}
for a given positive
v
satisfying Assumption
2.5
.

In the following we will write
\varphi
to mean a continuous function, that can change from one line to the next. We can now compute

\displaystyle(hu)(x)

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}y\,(hG)(x,y)v^{\varepsilon}(x-y)-\int\mathrm{d}y\,\nabla_{x}G(x,y)\cdot\nabla_{x}v^{\varepsilon}(x-y)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}y\,G(x-y)\Delta_{x}v^{\varepsilon}(x-y)\,

\displaystyle=v^{\varepsilon}(0)+\varphi(x)=\varphi(x)

where we used the fact that
(hG)(x,y)=\delta(x-y)
.
We have thus showed that
x\mapsto(hu)(x)
is a continuous function. We have

-(\Delta u)(x_{1},x_{2})+(\Delta u)(\tilde{x}_{1},\tilde{x}_{2})=2U(\tilde{x}_{1},\tilde{x}_{2})u(\tilde{x}_{1},\tilde{x}_{2})-2U(x_{1},x_{2})u(x_{1},x_{2})\\
+\varphi(x_{1},x_{2})-\varphi(\tilde{x}_{1},\tilde{x}_{2})\,.

Let
(a,b)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
be a point of discontinuity of
U
. Then

\limsup_{x_{1}\to a}\lvert-(\Delta u)(x_{1},b)+(\Delta u)(a,b)\rvert=2u(a,b){\Bigl(\limsup_{x_{1}\to a}\lvert U(x_{1},b)-U(a,b)\rvert\Bigr)}\neq 0\,

where we use the fact that
u
is a strictly positive function.
This contradicts the assumption that
u\in C^{2}
.
∎
